    *   **Adaptive Pretraining and Instruction Tuning:** This training strategy seems well-suited to equip the LLM with CAD-specific knowledge and adaptation capabilities.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:**  The paper presents convincing evidence that CAD-Llama outperforms baselines in various CAD-related tasks.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on GPT-4 for Annotation:** The annotation pipeline relies heavily on GPT-4, a proprietary model. This raises concerns about reproducibility and accessibility, limiting adoption in research settings where access to such models may be restricted.  Also, this reliance can introduce bias in the dataset.
    *   **Limited Exploration of LLM Architectures:** The paper focuses primarily on LLaMA3-8B. Exploring other LLM architectures or training strategies could further improve performance.
    *   **Complexity of the Framework:** The multi-stage process, involving VLM annotation, SPCC generation, pretraining, and instruction tuning, adds considerable complexity to the framework. This may pose a barrier to adoption and further development by other researchers.
    *   **Scalability:** The ability of CAD-Llama to handle *very* complex and large-scale CAD models is not extensively demonstrated.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence the field of CAD modeling by demonstrating the feasibility of using LLMs for parametric design. It could inspire further research in developing more intuitive and accessible CAD design tools, and it might change how designers work. It can also provide insight into how structured data and textual description can work in conjunction to solve the CAD task.

*   **Justification for Score:** Given the solid experimental results, the novelty in integrating different techniques, the potential for impact in the CAD modeling domain, and the identified weaknesses (especially the reliance on a proprietary model for annotation), a score of 7 seems appropriate. It's a significant step forward, but requires further refinement and broader adoption before realizing its full potential.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Efficient Flow Matching using Latent Variables
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04486v1)
- **Authors**: Anirban Samaddar, Yixuan Sun, Viktor Nilsson, Sandeep Madireddy
- **Abstract**: Flow matching models have shown great potential in image generation tasks among probabilistic generative models. Building upon the ideas of continuous normalizing flows, flow matching models generalize the transport path of the diffusion models from a simple prior distribution to the data. Most flow matching models in the literature do not explicitly model the underlying structure/manifold in the target data when learning the flow from a simple source distribution like the standard Gaussian. This leads to inefficient learning, especially for many high-dimensional real-world datasets, which often reside in a low-dimensional manifold. Existing strategies of incorporating manifolds, including data with underlying multi-modal distribution, often require expensive training and hence frequently lead to suboptimal performance. To this end, we present \texttt{Latent-CFM}, which provides simplified training/inference strategies to incorporate multi-modal data structures using pretrained deep latent variable models. Through experiments on multi-modal synthetic data and widely used image benchmark datasets, we show that \texttt{Latent-CFM} exhibits improved generation quality with significantly less training ($\sim 50\%$ less in some cases) and computation than state-of-the-art flow matching models. Using a 2d Darcy flow dataset, we demonstrate that our approach generates more physically accurate samples than competitive approaches. In addition, through latent space analysis, we demonstrate that our approach can be used for conditional image generation conditioned on latent features.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Efficient Flow Matching using Latent Variables":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Latent-CFM, a novel flow matching framework that leverages deep latent variable models (LVMs) to improve image generation. It addresses the limitation of standard flow matching (FM) methods that rely on simple source distributions, often leading to inefficient learning and suboptimal performance, especially for high-dimensional data residing on low-dimensional manifolds. Latent-CFM incorporates data structure by training or fine-tuning pre-trained LVMs (specifically VAEs and GMMs) to extract latent features of the target data. This enables conditional flow matching based on these latent features, streamlining the training and inference process. The authors demonstrate improved generation quality, reduced training time, and the ability to perform conditional image generation based on latent features through experiments on synthetic multi-modal datasets, image benchmark datasets (MNIST and CIFAR10), and a 2D Darcy flow dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The core idea of incorporating latent variable models to guide flow matching is novel and contributes to addressing the limitations of standard FM in handling structured data.  Previous works explored similar ideas, especially relating to GANs and diffusion, but applying this specifically to *conditional* flow matching with the efficiency gains shown is a differentiator. The key innovation is the *static encoder*, that allows generating data based on features learned from the data itself. The disentanglement benefits are also an interesting addition. The application to the Darcy Flow dataset and showing improvements in physical accuracy is a significant contribution compared to just demonstrating improved image FID scores.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a relevant and important problem in generative modeling: efficiently learning complex data distributions. By leveraging pre-trained LVMs, the method achieves better generation quality and faster training, making it a practical contribution. The application to Darcy flow shows potential to enhance scientific machine learning, by enabling the generation of physically plausible samples, which is a highly significant application area. The work helps bridge the gap between generative models and scientific data generation, opening doors for more robust and physically grounded simulations.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of standard FM.
    * **Well-defined Method:** Latent-CFM is clearly described, including the training and inference procedures.
    * **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the method on a variety of datasets, showing improved performance compared to state-of-the-art FM models. The visualizations are helpful in understanding the method's behavior.
    * **Application to Scientific Data:** Demonstrating the application on the 2D Darcy Flow data is a significant strength, showcasing its potential for real-world scientific applications.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependence on Pre-trained LVMs:** While leveraging pre-trained LVMs enhances efficiency, it also introduces dependence on the quality of the pre-trained model.  A poor pre-trained LVM could limit the performance of Latent-CFM.  The authors could explore and discuss the sensitivity of the performance to the choice of pre-trained LVM.
    * **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** Although diverse, the scope of the evaluation is limited to image and simplified scientific data. Exploring the method on other types of datasets (e.g., audio, text) could further demonstrate its generalizability.
    * **Lack of Theoretical Justification for Encoder Fine-Tuning Regularization:** The KL regularizer in Eq.15 is empirically motivated, but a more theoretical justification would strengthen the paper.
    * **Somewhat incremental improvement:**  While the results are promising, the improvement in FID isn't dramatically better than prior state-of-the-art on CIFAR10. A larger margin of improvement would have made the result even more impactful.

* **Potential Impact:**  Latent-CFM has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and robust flow-based generative models. The use of latent variables can facilitate the incorporation of domain knowledge, leading to better generation quality and improved interpretability. The application to scientific data also makes it valuable in scientific machine learning. It is also clear that the idea itself is quite simple, and could be easily adopted by other research groups.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7.

*   The paper offers a clearly articulated approach to a key problem in generative modeling and is well-executed.
*   The method (Latent-CFM) is novel and has clear advantages over standard flow matching, particularly in structured data and scientific applications.
*   The experimental results are solid and supportive of the claims made in the paper.
*   The application to Darcy flow enhances the significance of the work beyond simple image generation.
*   The dependence on the quality of pre-trained LVMs,  limits its broader use, and the relatively small improvement in some of the benchmark results compared to pre-existing work is a minor detractor.
*   A slightly higher theoretical analysis could make it an even stronger paper.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Defining and Quantifying Creative Behavior in Popular Image Generators
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04497v2)
- **Authors**: Aditi Ramaswamy, Hana Chockler, Melane Navaratnarajah
- **Abstract**: Creativity of generative AI models has been a subject of scientific debate in the last years, without a conclusive answer. In this paper, we study creativity from a practical perspective and introduce quantitative measures that help the user to choose a suitable AI model for a given task. We evaluated our measures on a number of popular image-to-image generation models, and the results of this suggest that our measures conform to human intuition.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper proposes a set of quantitative measures to assess the "task-oriented creativity" of popular `img2img` generative vision models.  It focuses on three aspects: satisfaction of prompt requirements, cohesion between output artifacts, and diversity of output artifacts. The authors introduce an iterative process inspired by the "telephone game" to generate chains of images, where each subsequent image is based on the previous one.  These chains are then analyzed using the proposed metrics. The paper tests the approach on popular models like Stable Diffusion 3, Kandinsky 2.2, and FLUX, analyzing the impact of temperature (or strength parameter) on creative performance. The results suggest that the proposed measures capture intuition about creative behavior and that image input temperature significantly impacts creativity.

**Critical Evaluation**

* **Novelty:**  The paper's core novelty lies in mathematically quantifying creativity in the context of `img2img` models and then using it to evaluate popular models. The iterative chain process for evaluating creative behavior is also a somewhat novel evaluation technique. Existing work typically focuses on text-to-image or large language models. Applying these concepts to `img2img` models fills a gap in the current literature. The use of iterative chains to simulate user interaction is a good idea.

* **Significance:** The significance is moderate. By providing quantitative metrics, the paper offers a more objective way to compare and evaluate generative models for specific creative tasks.  This is helpful for both users who want to choose the best model for their needs and for developers seeking to improve their models. The findings about the impact of input temperature are valuable for understanding how to control creative outputs. However, the reliance on object detection tools as a basis for evaluating quality, coherence, and diversity may limit their practical applicability because the models may miss important features of the images. The paper highlights the need to consider not only overall quality but also task-specific attributes when evaluating generative models, which is a valuable insight.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear definitions:** The paper provides clear and well-defined metrics for assessing creativity.
    * **Iterative evaluation:** The "telephone game" approach is a clever way to simulate real-world user interaction and evaluate how creativity evolves over time.
    * **Practical focus:** The emphasis on "task-oriented creativity" makes the work relevant to practical applications.
    * **Experimental validation:** The experiments provide evidence that the proposed measures correlate with intuitive notions of creativity.
    * **Code and data availability:** Openly sharing the code and data enhances reproducibility and allows others to build upon the work.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on object detection:** The reliance on object detection tools and word similarity may miss nuances in image content and context. The performance and biases of these underlying tools can directly affect the accuracy and reliability of the proposed metrics.
    * **Limited domain:** Experiments are limited to food images. Further validation across diverse image domains is needed to assess the generalizability of the metrics.
    * **Subjectivity:** While the measures are quantitative, the initial design and interpretation of the results still involve a degree of subjectivity in defining "creativity" and selecting appropriate evaluation tasks.
    * **Overreliance on temperature:** While it is a popular parameter in creative ML, is it really the right thing to change for a creative task? Would some other control parameter offer more creative control?
    * **T-tests:** T-tests are only appropriate where data can be shown to be normally distributed. Without demonstrating normality for each variable, the conclusions cannot be reached.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence how generative vision models are evaluated and compared in the future. The focus on task-oriented creativity and the provision of quantitative metrics could lead to the development of more targeted evaluation benchmarks. The findings on the impact of input temperature could guide developers in designing models with better creative control.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:**

The paper makes a moderate contribution to the field.  The quantification of creativity in `img2img` models and the use of iterative chains is novel and potentially useful.  The significance is reasonable, particularly for those interested in applying generative models for specific creative tasks. However, the reliance on object detection and word similarity tools, the limited domain of the experiments, and limited parameter usage reduces the impact of the work. The paper would be significantly strengthened by demonstrating generalizability across different datasets and tasks, as well as comparing the metrics to human evaluation scores. Without demonstration of normality, t-tests are inappropriate, so all analysis must be re-done for conclusions to be drawn.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Pangu Ultra MoE: How to Train Your Big MoE on Ascend NPUs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04519v1)
- **Authors**: Yehui Tang, Yichun Yin, Yaoyuan Wang, Hang Zhou, Yu Pan, Wei Guo, Ziyang Zhang, Miao Rang, Fangcheng Liu, Naifu Zhang, Binghan Li, Yonghan Dong, Xiaojun Meng, Yasheng Wang, Dong Li, Yin Li, Dandan Tu, Can Chen, Youliang Yan, Fisher Yu, Ruiming Tang, Yunhe Wang, Botian Huang, Bo Wang, Boxiao Liu, Changzheng Zhang, Da Kuang, Fei Liu, Gang Huang, Jiansheng Wei, Jiarui Qin, Jie Ran, Jinpeng Li, Jun Zhao, Liang Dai, Lin Li, Liqun Deng, Peifeng Qin, Pengyuan Zeng, Qiang Gu, Shaohua Tang, Shengjun Cheng, Tao Gao, Tao Yu, Tianshu Li, Tianyu Bi, Wei He, Weikai Mao, Wenyong Huang, Wulong Liu, Xiabing Li, Xianzhi Yu, Xueyu Wu, Xu He, Yangkai Du, Yan Xu, Ye Tian, Yimeng Wu, Yongbing Huang, Yong Tian, Yong Zhu, Yue Li, Yufei Wang, Yuhang Gai, Yujun Li, Yu Luo, Yunsheng Ni, Yusen Sun, Zelin Chen, Zhe Liu, Zhicheng Liu, Zhipeng Tu, Zilin Ding, Zongyuan Zhan
- **Abstract**: Sparse large language models (LLMs) with Mixture of Experts (MoE) and close to a trillion parameters are dominating the realm of most capable language models. However, the massive model scale poses significant challenges for the underlying software and hardware systems. In this paper, we aim to uncover a recipe to harness such scale on Ascend NPUs. The key goals are better usage of the computing resources under the dynamic sparse model structures and materializing the expected performance gain on the actual hardware. To select model configurations suitable for Ascend NPUs without repeatedly running the expensive experiments, we leverage simulation to compare the trade-off of various model hyperparameters. This study led to Pangu Ultra MoE, a sparse LLM with 718 billion parameters, and we conducted experiments on the model to verify the simulation results. On the system side, we dig into Expert Parallelism to optimize the communication between NPU devices to reduce the synchronization overhead. We also optimize the memory efficiency within the devices to further reduce the parameter and activation management overhead. In the end, we achieve an MFU of 30.0% when training Pangu Ultra MoE, with performance comparable to that of DeepSeek R1, on 6K Ascend NPUs, and demonstrate that the Ascend system is capable of harnessing all the training stages of the state-of-the-art language models. Extensive experiments indicate that our recipe can lead to efficient training of large-scale sparse language models with MoE. We also study the behaviors of such models for future reference.
- **Summary**: This technical report presents Pangu Ultra MoE, a 718-billion-parameter sparse large language model (LLM) trained on Huawei's Ascend NPUs. The paper focuses on strategies to improve system utilization and training efficiency of MoE models on the Ascend platform, addressing challenges like dynamic sparse model structures, communication overhead, high memory utilization, and imbalanced expert load. The methodology involves: 1) a simulation-based approach for architecture exploration to identify suitable MoE configurations for Ascend NPUs; 2) optimizing expert parallelism with a hierarchical all-to-all communication strategy; 3) improving memory efficiency using fine-grained recomputation and tensor swapping; and 4) dynamic device-level load balancing. The authors report achieving a 30.0% Model Flops Utilization (MFU) when training Pangu Ultra MoE on 6K Ascend NPUs, comparable to DeepSeek R1. The model is then evaluated on general language comprehension, reasoning, and industry-specific (medical) benchmarks, with results suggesting strong performance. The report also includes an MOE analysis to understand expert specialization, router scale, and expert co-activation.

**Evaluation:**

The paper offers a useful contribution by documenting the process of training a large-scale MoE model on the Ascend NPU platform. It provides a practical guide with specific engineering optimizations. The strengths of the paper lie in its detailed system optimizations and the comprehensive experimental evaluation. The use of simulation for model architecture search is a valuable technique for resource-constrained platforms. The specific optimizations for Ascend NPUs, such as the hierarchical all-to-all communication and fine-grained recomputation, address key performance bottlenecks. Furthermore, the ablation studies and MOE analysis offer insights into the behavior of sparse models and inform future research.

However, the novelty can be questioned. The individual optimization techniques (e.g., recomputation, tensor swapping, load balancing, parallelism strategies) are not entirely new to the field, although their combination and adaptation to the Ascend platform in the context of MoE training is novel. The paper also lacks a comparison with a wider range of existing MoE models and hardware platforms. The novelty is also somewhat diluted by the fact it's a technical report and not a peer-reviewed publication. While the paper showcases the capabilities of the Ascend platform and provides a recipe for efficient training, it does not present a fundamentally new approach to MoE training or model architecture. The reliance on proprietary hardware also limits the generalizability of some of the findings. The contribution could have been stronger if they open-sourced the optimized training code.

Given the combination of practical value, good experimental results, but also limited novelty, I would score it as follows:

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Comparative Analysis of Carbon Footprint in Manual vs. LLM-Assisted Code Development
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04521v1)
- **Authors**: Kuen Sum Cheung, Mayuri Kaul, Gunel Jahangirova, Mohammad Reza Mousavi, Eric Zie
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLM) have significantly transformed various domains, including software development. These models assist programmers in generating code, potentially increasing productivity and efficiency. However, the environmental impact of utilising these AI models is substantial, given their high energy consumption during both training and inference stages. This research aims to compare the energy consumption of manual software development versus an LLM-assisted approach, using Codeforces as a simulation platform for software development. The goal is to quantify the environmental impact and propose strategies for minimising the carbon footprint of using LLM in software development. Our results show that the LLM-assisted code generation leads on average to 32.72 higher carbon footprint than the manual one. Moreover, there is a significant correlation between task complexity and the difference in the carbon footprint of the two approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Comparative Analysis of Carbon Footprint in Manual vs. LLM-Assisted Code Development" investigates the environmental impact of using Large Language Models (LLMs) to assist in code development compared to traditional manual coding.  The authors used Codeforces as a platform to simulate both coding processes, analyzing energy consumption for tasks of varying complexity. Their findings indicate that LLM-assisted code generation, on average, results in a significantly higher carbon footprint than manual coding.  Furthermore, the difference in carbon footprint between the two approaches correlates positively with the complexity of the coding task. The paper concludes with recommendations for best practices to minimize the carbon footprint of LLM-assisted software development.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its quantitative analysis of the carbon footprint specifically comparing LLM-assisted *versus* manual coding, using a real-world programming contest platform for simulation. While previous research has addressed the general environmental impact of LLMs and the sustainability of software engineering practices, this study fills a crucial gap by providing a direct comparison within the context of code development. The observation that the carbon footprint difference increases with task complexity is also a novel and valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its direct relevance to the growing adoption of LLMs in software development. By quantifying the increased energy consumption, it highlights the need for careful consideration of environmental impact. The findings can inform decisions about when and how to best utilize LLMs in the software engineering process, promoting more sustainable development practices. The paper also contributes to the broader discussion on the ethical implications of AI by providing empirical data on its environmental costs. The identification of areas for optimization (like LLM query efficiency) is a practical contribution.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Quantitative Approach:** The paper provides a much-needed quantitative analysis, using metrics like energy consumption and carbon footprint calculation, providing a more concrete understanding of the problem.
    *   **Use of Codeforces:** Simulating the coding process on Codeforces is a strength. This is a reasonable proxy and provides access to real submission data.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper clearly outlines the methodology, including data collection, LLM interaction process, and carbon footprint estimation, facilitating reproducibility.
    *   **Detailed Carbon Footprint Calculation:** The breakdown of the carbon footprint into different components (e.g., coding, debugging, query) allows for a more granular analysis.
    *   **Addresses a real and growing concern:** Highlighting the environmental cost of AI tools is vital as these tools become increasingly integrated into software development workflows.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:**  The focus on a single platform (Codeforces) and a specific type of task (competitive programming problems) limits the generalizability of the findings. Real-world software development often involves more complex and collaborative projects.
    *   **Hardware Assumptions:**  The assumptions regarding hardware configurations (e.g., laptop TDP) and RAM usage introduce uncertainty. These are necessary simplifications, but should be carefully noted as limitations.  The lack of precise energy profiling and the reliance on estimates for query energy consumption are also a weakness.
    *   **LLM Prompting Strategy:**  Using a simple zero-shot prompting strategy might not represent the full potential of LLMs or best-practice LLM usage. More sophisticated prompting techniques or fine-tuning could yield different results. While it can be argued that simple prompting is 'fair', it doesn't reflect the diversity of application in the real world.
    *   **Limited Scope in the Life Cycle of Development:** It only considers a portion of the development life cycle such as testing, debugging, and implementation.

*   **Score Justification:**

    Given the novelty in providing a comparative quantitative analysis of carbon footprint between manual and LLM-assisted coding within a controlled simulation, its significance in informing sustainable AI practices in software engineering, and the clear methodology, the paper deserves a solid score. However, the limited scope, hardware assumptions, and simplifications in the LLM prompting strategy prevent a higher score. The lack of consideration for a real-world development lifecycle is also a major factor.

    Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### Text2CT: Towards 3D CT Volume Generation from Free-text Descriptions Using Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04522v1)
- **Authors**: Pengfei Guo, Can Zhao, Dong Yang, Yufan He, Vishwesh Nath, Ziyue Xu, Pedro R. A. S. Bassi, Zongwei Zhou, Benjamin D. Simon, Stephanie Anne Harmon, Baris Turkbey, Daguang Xu
- **Abstract**: Generating 3D CT volumes from descriptive free-text inputs presents a transformative opportunity in diagnostics and research. In this paper, we introduce Text2CT, a novel approach for synthesizing 3D CT volumes from textual descriptions using the diffusion model. Unlike previous methods that rely on fixed-format text input, Text2CT employs a novel prompt formulation that enables generation from diverse, free-text descriptions. The proposed framework encodes medical text into latent representations and decodes them into high-resolution 3D CT scans, effectively bridging the gap between semantic text inputs and detailed volumetric representations in a unified 3D framework. Our method demonstrates superior performance in preserving anatomical fidelity and capturing intricate structures as described in the input text. Extensive evaluations show that our approach achieves state-of-the-art results, offering promising potential applications in diagnostics, and data augmentation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Text2CT: Towards 3D CT Volume Generation from Free-text Descriptions Using Diffusion Model":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Text2CT, a novel 3D generative model for synthesizing CT volumes from free-text descriptions.  Unlike prior work that relies on fixed-format input, Text2CT leverages a new prompt formulation and large language models (LLMs) to encode diverse, free-text descriptions into a latent representation and then decode them into high-resolution 3D CT scans using a diffusion model. The framework integrates a 3D Compression Network to manage memory limitations and a text-conditional Latent Diffusion Model (LDM) for generation.  Experiments demonstrate superior performance in anatomical fidelity and capturing intricate structures compared to existing methods.  The paper highlights potential applications in diagnostics and data augmentation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the *combination* of several existing techniques into a unified framework for 3D CT volume generation from *free-text*.  The individual components – diffusion models, 3D compression networks (borrowed from MAISI), and leveraging LLMs for prompt engineering – are not themselves novel.  The adaptation of these techniques to medical imaging and the specific prompt formulation strategy contributes to the novelty.  The key is the end-to-end approach which overcomes limitations of previous methods like reliance on 2D super-resolution (GenerateCT) or low resolution output (MedSyn).

* **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing limitations of existing text-conditional medical image generation models.  The free-text input capability enhances real-world applicability and flexibility.  The high-resolution 3D volume generation is crucial for clinical relevance where fine anatomical details are essential. The data augmentation potential can mitigate data scarcity and privacy issues. This has the potential to create very large labeled synthetic datasets from text only, greatly improving the training of classifiers for diagnosis.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Unified 3D framework:**  Addresses memory limitations effectively and avoids artifacts introduced by 2D super-resolution-based approaches.
    * **Free-text Input:**  Significantly improves the model's adaptability and real-world usability compared to models with rigid input format requirements.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper provides both quantitative (FID, CLIP score) and qualitative (expert assessment) evaluations, including out-of-distribution performance, demonstrating robustness.  Ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components.
    * **Data Augmentation results:** Demonstrates that synthetic data can significantly improve performance of downstream classification models.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Component Novelty:**  The core components are not fundamentally new, but the *integration* is what drives the novelty. The paper relies on off-the-shelf 3D compression VAEs, reducing the contribution.
    * **Dependence on Text Quality:**  The model performance relies heavily on the quality and descriptiveness of the input text. While the LLM-based prompt formulation helps, it still requires sufficiently detailed descriptions. The prompt engineering for LLM generation is a bit basic, but is adequate for the current task.
    * **Computational Cost:**  Generating 3D volumes is computationally expensive, even with optimizations like the compression network. The runtime for generating a single volume is high.
    * **Limited Detail:** The specific instructions for prompting the LLM for a specific task is lacking in detail.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper can potentially influence research in several ways:
    * Encourage further work on text-conditional medical image generation.
    * Inspire new architectures for memory-efficient 3D generative models.
    * Provide a baseline for future research on free-text input for medical image generation.
    * Promote the use of synthetic data for medical image data augmentation.

The main weakness is the relatively incremental nature of the technical contribution, but the gains in performance and clinical applicability are considerable, meriting a high score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fight Fire with Fire: Defending Against Malicious RL Fine-Tuning via Reward Neutralization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04578v1)
- **Authors**: Wenjun Cao
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning transforms large language models while creating a vulnerability we experimentally verify: Our experiment shows that malicious RL fine-tuning dismantles safety guardrails with remarkable efficiency, requiring only 50 steps and minimal adversarial prompts, with harmful escalating from 0-2 to 7-9. This attack vector particularly threatens open-source models with parameter-level access. Existing defenses targeting supervised fine-tuning prove ineffective against RL's dynamic feedback mechanisms. We introduce Reward Neutralization, the first defense framework specifically designed against RL fine-tuning attacks, establishing concise rejection patterns that render malicious reward signals ineffective. Our approach trains models to produce minimal-information rejections that attackers cannot exploit, systematically neutralizing attempts to optimize toward harmful outputs. Experiments validate that our approach maintains low harmful scores (no greater than 2) after 200 attack steps, while standard models rapidly deteriorate. This work provides the first constructive proof that robust defense against increasingly accessible RL attacks is achievable, addressing a critical security gap for open-weight models.
- **Summary**: Okay, let's break down this paper.

**Summary:**

The paper "Fight Fire with Fire: Defending Against Malicious RL Fine-Tuning via Reward Neutralization" addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to malicious reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning attacks.  The authors demonstrate that such attacks can efficiently dismantle safety guardrails in LLMs, especially open-source models, requiring minimal data and optimization steps. They propose a defense mechanism called Reward Neutralization, which involves training models to produce concise, minimal-information rejections to harmful prompts. This makes it difficult for attackers to exploit reward signals and thereby reduces the model's susceptibility to RL-based attacks.  The authors validate their approach through experiments across multiple model architectures and harm domains, showing that Reward Neutralization can maintain low harmful scores even under sustained adversarial pressure, while standard models quickly deteriorate.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strength:** The paper's primary novelty lies in identifying and systematically addressing the specific vulnerabilities introduced by *RL fine-tuning* as an attack vector on LLMs.  While jailbreaking and adversarial attacks on LLMs are well-studied, the focus on RL fine-tuning as a means to dismantle safety guardrails is less explored. The Reward Neutralization approach itself, while not entirely novel in concept (defense through minimal information), is presented as a *specific adaptation and optimization* for the RL fine-tuning attack context. The idea of engineering parameter configurations that effectively eliminate exploitable reward differentials is a novel angle.
*   **Weakness:** Some aspects of the overall approach may draw inspiration from existing defense strategies against adversarial attacks (e.g., minimizing exploitable information, promoting robust refusals). The novelty is more in its application and optimization for this specific and increasingly relevant attack vector.

**Significance:**

*   **Strength:** The paper addresses a significant and growing threat to LLM security: the increasing accessibility of RL-based attack methods, particularly on open-source models where parameter-level access is available.  The experimental results demonstrate that the proposed Reward Neutralization approach effectively mitigates this threat, suggesting a viable path toward more robust defenses. The paper also performs both an analysis of the vulnerability and proposes the first defense strategy. Furthermore, the paper constructively demonstrated how to address the security issue.
*   **Weakness:** The evaluation, while sound, could be considered limited in scope. While the authors tested diverse model architectures and two harmful domains, expanding the experiments to include more models, domains, and different types of RL attacks would strengthen the claims of general effectiveness. Also, there is a claim of not claiming all possible RL attack scenarios which, although honest, may lead to concerns of how the methodology may generalise.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7.5** to this paper.

Here's why:

*   The identification of RL fine-tuning as a distinct and potent attack vector is a crucial contribution to the field of LLM security. This increases the awareness of the community.
*   Reward Neutralization offers a promising and specifically-tailored defense mechanism, representing a valuable addition to the growing toolkit of LLM security strategies. The defensive mechanism could also generalise.
*   The experimental results provide strong empirical evidence of the effectiveness of Reward Neutralization, demonstrating its ability to maintain safety boundaries under sustained adversarial pressure.
*   The paper does have some weaknesses. While the theoretical analysis and results make it a very promising defense, further validation regarding the defense's generalization capabilities could increase confidence in its robustness. Also, it's important to remember that security is always an "arms race," and future, more sophisticated RL attacks might still circumvent this defense.
*   The paper presents a well-structured, well-motivated, and empirically supported contribution that addresses a relevant and growing security concern in the field of LLMs.

The paper provides a constructive defense to the specific threat posed by malicious RL fine-tuning and will likely influence future research directions in the LLM security space.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### SlideItRight: Using AI to Find Relevant Slides and Provide Feedback for Open-Ended Questions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04584v1)
- **Authors**: Chloe Qianhui Zhao, Jie Cao, Eason Chen, Kenneth R. Koedinger, Jionghao Lin
- **Abstract**: Feedback is important in supporting student learning. While various automated feedback systems have been implemented to make the feedback scalable, many existing solutions only focus on generating text-based feedback. As is indicated in the multimedia learning principle, learning with more modalities could help utilize more separate channels, reduce the cognitive load and facilitate students' learning. Hence, it is important to explore the potential of Artificial Intelligence (AI) in feedback generation from and to different modalities. Our study leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) for textual feedback with the supplementary guidance from other modality - relevant lecture slide retrieved from the slides hub. Through an online crowdsourcing study (N=91), this study investigates learning gains and student perceptions using a 2x2 design (i.e., human feedback vs. AI feedback and with vs. without relevant slide), evaluating the clarity, engagement, perceived effectiveness, and reliability) of AI-facilitated multimodal feedback. We observed significant pre-to-post learning gains across all conditions. However, the differences in these gains were not statistically significant between conditions. The post-survey revealed that students found the slide feedback helpful in their learning process, though they reported difficulty in understanding it. Regarding the AI-generated open-ended feedback, students considered it personalized and relevant to their responses, but they expressed lower trust in the AI feedback compared to human-generated feedback.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SlideItRight: Using AI to Find Relevant Slides and Provide Feedback for Open-Ended Questions" explores the potential of using AI, specifically Large Language Models (LLMs), to generate feedback for students in online learning environments. The key innovation is a system that combines AI-generated textual feedback with relevant lecture slides retrieved from a slide repository. The study investigates the impact of this multimodal feedback approach (AI + slides) on student learning gains and perceptions through a 2x2 crowdsourcing experiment.  The factors tested are human vs. AI feedback and with vs. without slide inclusion. The study measures learning gains using pre/post-tests and assesses student perceptions of clarity, engagement, effectiveness, and usability through surveys. Results show significant learning gains across all conditions, but no statistically significant differences *between* the conditions.  Students generally found the slide feedback helpful, although understanding it presented challenges. Students considered AI feedback personalized but exhibited lower trust in AI-generated feedback compared to human feedback.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of LLM-generated textual feedback with retrieval of relevant instructional slides for open-ended questions shows some novelty. While LLMs have been applied to feedback generation previously, the integration of content retrieval from course slides to ground and contextualize the feedback is a valuable extension. It attempts to address the LLM "hallucination" problem and cognitive overload associated with too much text by linking feedback to visual teaching material. The idea of using OpenAI Vision API to 'understand' the slides is also a positive contribution.
*   **Significance:** The research question is important: how can AI effectively enhance learning and what is the impact on learner perceptions? Providing personalized and timely feedback is a key challenge in online education, particularly in high student-teacher ratio environments. The findings highlight the potential of AI to provide scalable, personalized feedback but also reveal important limitations in terms of trust, clarity, and potential cognitive overload.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined research questions:** The study addresses clear questions about the impact of AI-facilitated multimodal feedback on learning gains and student perceptions.
    *   **Experimental Design:** The 2x2 design allows for a comparison of different feedback modalities.
    *   **Implementation details are clearly explained**: the use of GPT-4 Vision, retrieval augmented generation and design of prompts are well explained.
    *   **Focus on perceptions**: the paper includes a strong investigation on learners' perceptions of the AI feedback which are valuable for future improvements of the system.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Lack of statistically significant differences:**  The main finding, that there were no statistically significant differences in learning gains between the conditions, weakens the impact of the study.  While trends were observed, the lack of statistical support suggests that the effect of the AI-facilitated multimodal feedback might not be substantial.
    *   **Sample Size and Generalizability:** While the sample size is respectable for a crowdsourcing study, the single-domain context limits the generalizability of the findings. Also, participants recruited online are known to bias the study.
    *   **Clarity of Slide Feedback:** The study notes that students found the slide feedback difficult to understand, limiting its effectiveness. A clearer integration of slide content into the overall feedback strategy could enhance its utility. The authors also noted the AI overgeneralization; "The system did was acknowledge my answer and say 'but there's more you could do'" which leads to lower trust in AI system.
    *   **Trust issues**: The AI-generated feedback received less trust compared to the human-written feedback. The system does not fully adopt a learner-centered structure.

*   **Potential Influence:** Despite its limitations, the study contributes to the growing body of research on the use of AI in education. The findings provide valuable insights into the challenges and opportunities of using LLMs to generate personalized feedback and the importance of considering student perceptions and cognitive load. It prompts further research into how to design AI-facilitated feedback systems that are both effective and trustworthy. Further work would need to be done to fully adopt learner-centered approaches.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel approach to AI-driven feedback generation, but the lack of statistically significant differences in learning gains significantly diminishes its impact. While the study provides valuable insights into student perceptions and highlights important design considerations, the limited generalizability and the identified limitations in trust and clarity constrain its overall contribution. The study, however, is still valuable and serves as a proof of concept for future studies.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### ZeroSearch: Incentivize the Search Capability of LLMs without Searching
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04588v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Sun, Zile Qiao, Jiayan Guo, Xuanbo Fan, Yingyan Hou, Yong Jiang, Pengjun Xie, Fei Huang, Yan Zhang
- **Abstract**: Effective information searching is essential for enhancing the reasoning and generation capabilities of large language models (LLMs). Recent research has explored using reinforcement learning (RL) to improve LLMs' search capabilities by interacting with live search engines in real-world environments. While these approaches show promising results, they face two major challenges: (1) Uncontrolled Document Quality: The quality of documents returned by search engines is often unpredictable, introducing noise and instability into the training process. (2) Prohibitively High API Costs: RL training requires frequent rollouts, potentially involving hundreds of thousands of search requests, which incur substantial API expenses and severely constrain scalability. To address these challenges, we introduce ZeroSearch, a reinforcement learning framework that incentivizes the search capabilities of LLMs without interacting with real search engines. Our approach begins with lightweight supervised fine-tuning to transform the LLM into a retrieval module capable of generating both relevant and noisy documents in response to a query. During RL training, we employ a curriculum-based rollout strategy that incrementally degrades the quality of generated documents, progressively eliciting the model's reasoning ability by exposing it to increasingly challenging retrieval scenarios. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ZeroSearch effectively incentivizes the search capabilities of LLMs using a 3B LLM as the retrieval module. Remarkably, a 7B retrieval module achieves comparable performance to the real search engine, while a 14B retrieval module even surpasses it. Furthermore, it generalizes well across both base and instruction-tuned models of various parameter sizes and is compatible with a wide range of RL algorithms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ZEROSEARCH, a novel reinforcement learning (RL) framework designed to improve the search capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) without relying on real-time interaction with external search engines. ZEROSEARCH addresses two key limitations of prior RL-based approaches: unpredictable document quality from live search engines and high API costs.  The approach involves: (1) Supervised fine-tuning an LLM to act as a retrieval module that can generate both relevant and noisy documents based on a query; and (2) Curriculum learning during RL training, where the quality of the generated documents is progressively degraded, forcing the policy model to reason more effectively in increasingly challenging retrieval scenarios.  Experiments demonstrate that ZEROSEARCH can incentivize search capabilities using a smaller LLM as the retrieval module, and larger retrieval modules can even outperform real search engines. The framework is shown to be generalizable across base and instruction-tuned models and compatible with various RL algorithms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a compelling approach to a significant problem in LLM research: improving access to external knowledge without incurring high costs or suffering from unreliable document quality. Here's a breakdown of the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses:

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of simulating a search engine using a lightweight fine-tuned LLM and then using curriculum learning to train a policy model is relatively novel.  While RL for improving search is not new, the way they address the cost and quality issues using simulated search and curriculum learning is a substantial contribution. The approach of fine-tuning the LLM to be a controllable document generator (useful and noisy) is also interesting and facilitates the curriculum learning aspect.

*   **Significance:** RAG is a very important topic. Overcoming its limitations (specifically, cost and noise due to online searching) has direct real-world application. ZEROSEARCH directly contributes to making retrieval-augmented LLMs more practical. The results demonstrated are also impressive, showing that a simulated approach can match or even surpass the performance of live search.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a clear problem:** The paper directly tackles the practical challenges of API costs and document quality associated with RL-based search improvement for LLMs.
    *   **Well-designed approach:** The use of supervised fine-tuning for search simulation and curriculum learning provides a systematic and effective way to train the policy model.
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The experiments are thorough, comparing ZEROSEARCH against multiple baselines across several datasets and model types. The performance gains and generalization ability are well-demonstrated.
    *   **Scalability:** The approach has shown good results in scaling to very large LLMs with significant speed up.
    *   **Reproducibility/Practicality**: The framework appears relatively easy to implement and integrates well with established RL algorithms.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on SFT Data Quality:** The performance of the simulation LLM hinges on the quality of the supervised fine-tuning data, which needs to be carefully labeled (useful vs. noisy). Although they outlined how the SFT data is collected and labeled, its success is crucial to the entire approach.
    *   **Simulated vs. Real World:** While the experiments show strong performance, there's always a gap between simulated environments and the complexity of real-world web search. This needs to be acknowledged, although the results that match/exceed online search engines somewhat alleviate the concern.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Failure Cases**: While the paper demonstrates significant performance gains, it would benefit from a more detailed analysis of failure cases, providing insights into the limitations of the approach.
    *   **Potential resource cost:** The implementation is cheaper than querying the API but still depends on a high end GPU to conduct its training.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in several ways:
    *   **Encouraging simulated environments for RL training:** The success of ZEROSEARCH could inspire more researchers to explore simulation-based approaches for training LLMs.
    *   **Curriculum learning for retrieval:** The curriculum learning strategy could be adopted and refined in other retrieval-augmented LLM settings.
    *   **Low-cost and controllable knowledge integration:** The framework offers a viable alternative to expensive and unpredictable API-based approaches.

**Overall:**

ZEROSEARCH offers a significant and practical advance in improving LLM search capabilities. The approach is well-designed, addresses a clear need, and demonstrates strong empirical results. The paper provides a strong case for simulation-based RL training, offering a compelling alternative to API-dependent methods. While some limitations exist (simulation realism, SFT data sensitivity), the overall contribution is substantial.

Score: 8. This score reflects the novelty of the approach, its significance in addressing key limitations of RAG, the rigor of the experimental evaluation, and the potential for influencing future research. The weaknesses mainly relate to potential implementation challenges in specific scenarios, rather than fundamental flaws in the design.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### MonoCoP: Chain-of-Prediction for Monocular 3D Object Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04594v2)
- **Authors**: Zhihao Zhang, Abhinav Kumar, Girish Chandar Ganesan, Xiaoming Liu
- **Abstract**: Accurately predicting 3D attributes is crucial for monocular 3D object detection (Mono3D), with depth estimation posing the greatest challenge due to the inherent ambiguity in mapping 2D images to 3D space. While existing methods leverage multiple depth cues (e.g., estimating depth uncertainty, modeling depth error) to improve depth accuracy, they overlook that accurate depth prediction requires conditioning on other 3D attributes, as these attributes are intrinsically inter-correlated through the 3D to 2D projection, which ultimately limits overall accuracy and stability. Inspired by Chain-of-Thought (CoT) in large language models (LLMs), this paper proposes MonoCoP, which leverages a Chain-of-Prediction (CoP) to predict attributes sequentially and conditionally via three key designs. First, it employs a lightweight AttributeNet (AN) for each 3D attribute to learn attribute-specific features. Next, MonoCoP constructs an explicit chain to propagate these learned features from one attribute to the next. Finally, MonoCoP uses a residual connection to aggregate features for each attribute along the chain, ensuring that later attribute predictions are conditioned on all previously processed attributes without forgetting the features of earlier ones. Experimental results show that our MonoCoP achieves state-of-the-art (SoTA) performance on the KITTI leaderboard without requiring additional data and further surpasses existing methods on the Waymo and nuScenes frontal datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MonoCoP: Chain-of-Prediction for Monocular 3D Object Detection" addresses the challenges in monocular 3D object detection, particularly the difficulty of accurate depth estimation due to the inherent ambiguity of projecting 3D information onto a 2D image.  The key idea is that accurate depth prediction requires conditioning on other 3D attributes (size, angle) because they are inter-correlated. The authors propose MonoCoP, a Chain-of-Prediction (CoP) framework.  MonoCoP predicts 3D attributes sequentially and conditionally through: (1) AttributeNets (AN) to learn attribute-specific features, (2) an explicit chain for propagating features between attributes, and (3) residual connections for aggregating features along the chain. Experimental results show state-of-the-art performance on KITTI, Waymo, and nuScenes frontal datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the application of a chain-of-prediction approach, inspired by Chain-of-Thought (CoT) from large language models, to the monocular 3D object detection problem.  While CoT has been used in other vision tasks, this is a novel adaptation for 3D attribute prediction. The explicit modeling of inter-attribute dependencies and the sequential prediction scheme are key contributions. The AttributeNet, feature propagation, and feature aggregation mechanisms, while relatively simple individually, contribute to a coherent and effective framework.  The mathematical illustration of inter-attribute correlations is a solid, though not ground-breaking, analytical contribution.

* **Significance:** The significance is demonstrated through the consistent state-of-the-art results on several challenging datasets (KITTI, Waymo, nuScenes).  The improvement in performance, especially for distant objects (where depth estimation is notoriously difficult), is compelling. The ablation studies provide good evidence that each component of MonoCoP (AN, feature propagation, feature aggregation) contributes to the overall performance.  The paper addresses a key problem in monocular 3D detection—the inaccurate and unstable depth estimates resulting from the ill-posed nature of the problem and the neglected correlations between attributes.  The gains, though, are incremental relative to other leading methods, so while SOTA, it's not a revolutionary change. The ablations studying the effect of different backbones contribute positively.  The inclusion of experiments using ResNet backbones contributes to robustness and applicability of the proposed solution.

* **Strengths:**
    * Strong empirical results across multiple datasets.
    * Well-designed ablation studies isolating the impact of each component.
    * Clear explanation of the proposed framework.
    * Explicit modeling and analysis of inter-attribute dependencies.
    * Good handling of potential error propagation (aggregation and residual connections)

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The individual components (AttributeNet, feature propagation, residual connections) are not deeply novel techniques themselves. The novelty comes in their specific *combination* and application to the 3D detection task.
    * It is unclear how dependent the technique is on the *specific* order of attributes in the chain, and how adaptable it is when new attributes get added. The ablation study provides some insight, but this could be explored further.
    * The ablation on the use of chained vs individual predictors is somewhat weak (shows mostly the same performance).
    * The comparison to hierarchical task learning and CoOp for the same task in the ablation is very interesting and strengthens the paper.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future work on monocular 3D object detection.  The idea of explicitly modeling inter-attribute dependencies and using a sequential prediction approach is likely to be adopted and extended by other researchers. The framework provides a solid foundation for future exploration of more sophisticated attribute interactions.

* **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a novel and effective approach to a significant problem in computer vision (monocular 3D object detection). While the individual components are not fundamentally new, their combination and application within the chain-of-prediction framework are innovative and yield state-of-the-art results on multiple challenging datasets. The thorough ablation studies support the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The paper's primary weakness lies in the incremental nature of the improvements and that it doesn't introduce extremely novel *methods* from a methods perspective. Nevertheless, given its significance and the extent and rigor of the validation, a solid score is warranted.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### OmniGIRL: A Multilingual and Multimodal Benchmark for GitHub Issue Resolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04606v1)
- **Authors**: Lianghong Guo, Wei Tao, Runhan Jiang, Yanlin Wang, Jiachi Chen, Xilin Liu, Yuchi Ma, Mingzhi Mao, Hongyu Zhang, Zibin Zheng
- **Abstract**: The GitHub issue resolution task aims to resolve issues reported in repositories automatically. With advances in large language models (LLMs), this task has gained increasing attention, and several benchmarks are proposed to evaluate the issue resolution ability of LLMs. However, existing benchmarks have three main limitations. First, current benchmarks focus on a single programming language, limiting the evaluation of issues from repositories across different languages. Second, they usually cover a narrow range of domains, which may fail to represent the diversity of real-world issues. Third, existing benchmarks rely solely on textual information in issue descriptions, overlooking multimodal information such as images in issues. In this paper, we propose OmniGIRL, a GitHub Issue ResoLution benchmark that is multilingual, multimodal, and multi-domain. OmniGIRL includes 959 task instances, which are collected from repositories across four programming languages (i.e., Python, JavaScript, TypeScript, and Java) and eight different domains. Our evaluation shows that current LLMs show limited performances on OmniGIRL. Notably, the best-performing model, GPT-4o, resolves only 8.6% of the issues. Besides, we find that current LLMs struggle to resolve issues requiring understanding images. The best performance is achieved by Claude-3.5-Sonnet, which resolves only 10.5% of the issues with image information. Finally, we analyze the reasons behind current LLMs' failure on OmniGIRL, providing insights for future improvements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OmniGIRL, a new benchmark for evaluating the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to resolve GitHub issues.  It addresses limitations in existing benchmarks by being multilingual (Python, JavaScript, TypeScript, Java), multimodal (incorporating textual descriptions, images, and website links), and covering a diverse set of repository domains. The authors evaluate state-of-the-art LLMs on OmniGIRL, revealing limited performance, particularly on issues requiring understanding of images and cross-file modifications.  They analyze the reasons for LLM failures, pointing to challenges in output formatting, handling cross-file edits, and utilizing visual information.  The benchmark, data collection code, and tutorial are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Real Gap:** The paper correctly identifies and addresses significant gaps in the current landscape of GitHub issue resolution benchmarks. Existing benchmarks often focus on a single language, a limited range of domains, and primarily rely on textual information. OmniGIRL's multilingual, multimodal, and multi-domain nature makes it a more realistic and comprehensive evaluation tool.

*   **Thorough Benchmark Creation:** The paper provides a clear and detailed explanation of the OmniGIRL construction process, including language/repository selection, data collection, task instance construction, and filtering steps. This transparency enhances the reproducibility and trustworthiness of the benchmark.

*   **Multimodal aspect** is a good step to represent issues close to real world scenarios. Including website links and images are promising ways to enrich the issue representation.

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation and Analysis:** The authors conduct a thorough evaluation of multiple LLMs using different methods, providing valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses. The failure analysis is particularly useful, identifying specific challenges faced by LLMs.

*   **Practical Contribution:** The availability of the benchmark, code, and tutorial will encourage further research in this area and facilitate the development of more effective issue resolution techniques.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Image Set:** While the inclusion of images is a significant improvement, the number of instances with images (only 19) is relatively small. This limits the scope of the analysis on multimodal issue resolution. The relatively small size of the image issue subset may result in statistically unstable findings.

*   **Website Link Evaluation Absent:** The paper notes the presence of website links within issue descriptions but doesn't evaluate any methods using this information. This represents a missed opportunity to explore a crucial aspect of multimodality.

*   **Dependency on Gold Patches:** While the oracle retrieval method provides valuable insights in an ideal setting, it's not directly applicable in real-world scenarios where the correct file location is unknown. The practical applicability of the LLMs evaluated in this way is limited.

*   **Unclear Benefit of LLM agents:** The advantage of multi-agent or automated retrieval over simple retrieval mechanism is not properly emphasized.

*   **Choice of evaluation metrics** While the report does mention the Cost metric, the paper would be strengthened by including an efficiency analysis based on the cost parameter to improve the report.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's novelty lies in the creation of the OmniGIRL benchmark itself, with its unique combination of multilingual, multimodal, and multi-domain characteristics. This benchmark is a significant contribution to the field of automated software engineering, specifically GitHub issue resolution. It allows for a more realistic and comprehensive evaluation of LLMs than existing benchmarks. The findings from the evaluation and analysis provide valuable insights into the challenges and opportunities in this area.

The significance of the work lies in its potential to drive the development of more effective and robust issue resolution techniques. By identifying the limitations of current LLMs, the paper points the way towards future research directions, such as improved handling of multimodal data, cross-file modifications, and language-specific code structures.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper addresses an important problem and offers a significant improvement over existing benchmarks. The creation of OmniGIRL is a substantial undertaking, and the evaluation and analysis provide valuable insights. However, the limitations in image representation, evaluation of webpage data, and reliance on oracle retrieval limit the immediate impact and practical applicability of the findings.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Score Distillation Sampling for Audio: Source Separation, Synthesis, and Beyond
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04621v1)
- **Authors**: Jessie Richter-Powell, Antonio Torralba, Jonathan Lorraine
- **Abstract**: We introduce Audio-SDS, a generalization of Score Distillation Sampling (SDS) to text-conditioned audio diffusion models. While SDS was initially designed for text-to-3D generation using image diffusion, its core idea of distilling a powerful generative prior into a separate parametric representation extends to the audio domain. Leveraging a single pretrained model, Audio-SDS enables a broad range of tasks without requiring specialized datasets. In particular, we demonstrate how Audio-SDS can guide physically informed impact sound simulations, calibrate FM-synthesis parameters, and perform prompt-specified source separation. Our findings illustrate the versatility of distillation-based methods across modalities and establish a robust foundation for future work using generative priors in audio tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Audio-SDS, a generalization of Score Distillation Sampling (SDS) for text-conditioned audio diffusion models. Unlike previous uses of SDS focused on 3D generation using image diffusion, Audio-SDS adapts the core idea of distilling a generative prior into a separate parametric representation to the audio domain. This allows for tasks like guiding physically informed impact sound simulations, calibrating FM-synthesis parameters, and performing prompt-specified source separation using a single pretrained model without requiring specialized datasets. The authors highlight three key improvements to the original SDS approach for audio: a decoder-based SDS to avoid differentiating through latent encoders, spectrogram emphasis to better capture audio realism, and multi-step denoising for a more stable guidance signal.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of extending SDS to audio is novel. While SDS is established in image and 3D generation, its application to audio opens up new avenues. The specific adaptations made for audio (decoder-based SDS, spectrogram emphasis, and multi-step denoising) demonstrate a practical understanding of audio challenges and are not merely trivial adaptations. Applying SDS to FM-synthesis and physically-informed simulations are also reasonable and non-obvious. The source separation application, leveraging text prompts is particularly appealing in its potential for interactive editing.

* **Significance:** The paper demonstrates a versatile framework with potential impact in several audio-related areas. It showcases how a single pretrained model can be used for diverse tasks without retraining, reducing the need for task-specific datasets. This could lower the barrier to entry for audio generation and editing tasks. The physical simulation and source separation applications show ways to integrate interpretable parametric models with powerful generative priors, enabling a user to guide the process through prompts while maintaining parametric control.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Versatility:** The framework can be used for a surprisingly wide variety of audio tasks using the same underlying pretrained diffusion model.
    * **Practical adaptations:** The decoder-based SDS, spectrogram emphasis, and multi-step denoising make SDS work well for audio.
    * **Clear presentation:** The paper is relatively well-written and explains the method and experiments clearly.
    * **Good qualitative results:** The examples of guided FM synthesis and impact sound generation are promising.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited quantitative evaluation:** The evaluation relies mostly on CLAP score, which is a measure of text-audio alignment. Direct comparisons with baselines (where applicable) using established audio metrics (e.g. for source separation beyond just SDR) would be stronger. The paper states that the audio diffusion models are not trained on task-specific datasets which makes direct comparisons difficult but not impossible.
    * **Dependency on a pretrained audio diffusion model:**  The method's performance is inherently limited by the capabilities of the chosen pretrained model (Stable Audio Open).  While the open-source nature of this model is a plus for reproducibility, its quality may not be state-of-the-art.  This dependency is not necessarily a drawback, but it's a constraint worth acknowledging.
    * **Computational cost:** The compute requirements are non-trivial, indicating that this is not a light-weight approach.
    * **Failure cases:** Although discussed, the failure cases are important.  Out-of-distribution prompts, long sequences, and parametric model mismatches limit the approach. This also indicates reliance on existing diffusion model biases and capabilities.
    * **Incremental advance:** Given recent advancements in diffusion-based generative models, the paper's improvements can be seen as an incremental advance on established techniques. While the authors present significant qualitative examples, a more compelling evaluation with quantitative comparisons would elevate the novelty beyond merely an innovative application.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper can potentially influence the audio research community by demonstrating the power of SDS for audio tasks. The presented framework can serve as a starting point for future work on generative audio modeling, audio editing, and audio-visual integration. The specific adaptations for audio could be adopted in other SDS-based applications. The impact, however, will depend on whether the community builds upon this work and addresses the limitations identified above.

* **Rigorous Rationale:** While the application of SDS to audio is a worthwhile contribution, the evaluation and some of the challenges with audio-specific implementations hold it back. Demonstrating strong quantitative results (beyond alignment) and exploring more ways to overcome the failure cases, the work has the potential to have a far greater impact.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### Retrieval Augmented Generation Evaluation for Health Documents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04680v1)
- **Authors**: Mario Ceresa, Lorenzo Bertolini, Valentin Comte, Nicholas Spadaro, Barbara Raffael, Brigitte Toussaint, Sergio Consoli, Amalia Muñoz Piñeiro, Alex Patak, Maddalena Querci, Tobias Wiesenthal
- **Abstract**: Safe and trustworthy use of Large Language Models (LLM) in the processing of healthcare documents and scientific papers could substantially help clinicians, scientists and policymakers in overcoming information overload and focusing on the most relevant information at a given moment. Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) is a promising method to leverage the potential of LLMs while enhancing the accuracy of their outcomes. This report assesses the potentials and shortcomings of such approaches in the automatic knowledge synthesis of different types of documents in the health domain. To this end, it describes: (1) an internally developed proof of concept pipeline that employs state-of-the-art practices to deliver safe and trustable analysis for healthcare documents and scientific papers called RAGEv (Retrieval Augmented Generation Evaluation); (2) a set of evaluation tools for LLM-based document retrieval and generation; (3) a benchmark dataset to verify the accuracy and veracity of the results called RAGEv-Bench. It concludes that careful implementations of RAG techniques could minimize most of the common problems in the use of LLMs for document processing in the health domain, obtaining very high scores both on short yes/no answers and long answers. There is a high potential for incorporating it into the day-to-day work of policy support tasks, but additional efforts are required to obtain a consistent and trustworthy tool.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided document:

**Summary:**

The report presents a study on Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) techniques for processing health-related documents using Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors developed a proof-of-concept pipeline called RAGEv (Retrieval Augmented Generation Evaluation) and a benchmark dataset, RAGEv-Bench, to evaluate the performance of RAG methods in this domain. They examine various strategies for improving the accuracy of LLM-generated answers, focusing on indexing, embeddings, retrieval, and generation within the RAG framework. The report includes automatic performance evaluation (APE) using PubMedQA and manual usability checks on datasets related to Horizon research, virtual human twins, and bacteriophages. The findings suggest that RAG techniques can minimize common problems associated with using LLMs for health document processing, resulting in high scores for both short and long answers. The report also highlights the potential for incorporating RAG into policy support tasks, while emphasizing the need for ongoing efforts to ensure consistency and trustworthiness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the application of RAG to health documents isn't entirely new, the report provides a comprehensive evaluation framework and a usable tool. The RAGEv pipeline and RAGEv-Bench dataset represent a tangible contribution.  The exploration of different RAG configurations (e.g., SHy pipeline) and their impact on usability across different use cases is valuable. The inclusion of usability testing with domain experts is a noteworthy addition, addressing the practical challenges of implementing LLMs in real-world settings.  The critical assessment of different embedding methods and retrieval strategies contributes to the existing body of knowledge but does not constitute a major breakthrough.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in demonstrating the potential of RAG to improve the reliability and trustworthiness of LLMs in the health domain. By addressing challenges like hallucination and bias, RAGEv aims to promote safer and more effective use of LLMs in healthcare. The emphasis on factuality and explainability is crucial for gaining the trust of healthcare professionals and policymakers. The usability testing is particularly significant as it provides practical insights into the challenges and opportunities of integrating LLMs into existing workflows.  The development of RAGEv-Bench enables reproducible research and facilitates comparison of different RAG implementations, which is a valuable contribution.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive evaluation framework: RAGEv and RAGEv-Bench provide a valuable resource for assessing RAG performance in the health domain.
    *   Usability testing: The inclusion of manual usability checks with domain experts offers practical insights into the real-world application of RAG.
    *   Detailed analysis: The report provides a detailed analysis of various RAG components and their impact on performance.
    *   Clear identification of limitations: The authors acknowledge the limitations of RAGEv and identify areas for future work.
    *   Addresses trustworthiness: The focus on factuality, explainability, and bias mitigation contributes to building trust in LLM-based healthcare solutions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited scope: The study focuses on a specific set of datasets and RAG configurations.
    *   Scalability concerns: The authors acknowledge that RAGEv is not scalable enough to be used as a full Information System.
    *   Statistical analysis: The depth of the statistical analysis could be more robust. While they employ ANOVA and LMM, a more detailed examination of interaction effects and statistical significance might strengthen the conclusions.
    *   Generalizability: While they use several cases, the study of the results on different cases could have given more insight into the generalization possibilities and the specific benefits on each application.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence the development and implementation of RAG-based solutions for healthcare. The RAGEv pipeline and RAGEv-Bench dataset can serve as a benchmark for future research and development efforts.
