The usability testing insights can guide the design of more user-friendly and effective LLM-based tools for healthcare professionals and policymakers.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a notable contribution by providing a tangible tool (RAGEv) and dataset (RAGEv-Bench) for evaluating RAG in the healthcare domain. The inclusion of usability testing provides valuable practical insights. However, it lacks significant theoretical breakthroughs and has some limitations in scope and statistical rigor. Taking these factors into consideration, the score is 7.

### Lay-Your-Scene: Natural Scene Layout Generation with Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04718v1)
- **Authors**: Divyansh Srivastava, Xiang Zhang, He Wen, Chenru Wen, Zhuowen Tu
- **Abstract**: We present Lay-Your-Scene (shorthand LayouSyn), a novel text-to-layout generation pipeline for natural scenes. Prior scene layout generation methods are either closed-vocabulary or use proprietary large language models for open-vocabulary generation, limiting their modeling capabilities and broader applicability in controllable image generation. In this work, we propose to use lightweight open-source language models to obtain scene elements from text prompts and a novel aspect-aware diffusion Transformer architecture trained in an open-vocabulary manner for conditional layout generation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LayouSyn outperforms existing methods and achieves state-of-the-art performance on challenging spatial and numerical reasoning benchmarks. Additionally, we present two applications of LayouSyn. First, we show that coarse initialization from large language models can be seamlessly combined with our method to achieve better results. Second, we present a pipeline for adding objects to images, demonstrating the potential of LayouSyn in image editing applications.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Lay-Your-Scene: Natural Scene Layout Generation with Diffusion Transformers":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LayouSyn, a novel text-to-layout generation pipeline designed for natural scenes. It addresses limitations in previous scene layout generation methods, which are often closed-vocabulary or rely on proprietary large language models (LLMs). LayouSyn utilizes lightweight open-source language models to extract scene elements from text prompts.  The core of the approach is a novel aspect-aware diffusion Transformer architecture trained in an open-vocabulary manner for conditional layout generation. The authors demonstrate LayouSyn's superior performance in generating plausible scene layouts and achieving state-of-the-art results on spatial and numerical reasoning benchmarks.  They also showcase applications, including combining LayouSyn with LLM-based coarse initialization and adding objects to existing images.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a crucial problem in controllable image generation: the need for high-quality, easily generated scene layouts from text prompts. The focus on open-source components is a significant strength, enhancing accessibility and reproducibility. The aspect-aware diffusion Transformer is also a novel contribution.

Here's a breakdown of strengths and weaknesses:

**Strengths:**

*   **Open-Source Focus:**  The use of lightweight, open-source language models and a diffusion transformer architecture is a significant advantage. This makes the method more accessible and reproducible than approaches reliant on proprietary LLMs. It promotes wider adoption and further research.
*   **Aspect-Aware Design:** Incorporating aspect ratio awareness into the diffusion model is a sensible and potentially impactful design choice for generating realistic layouts. It addresses a known issue with naive bounding box generation approaches.
*   **Strong Results:** The paper demonstrates quantitatively that LayouSyn outperforms existing methods on challenging benchmarks, particularly in spatial and numerical reasoning. Qualitative results show more plausible layouts, which is subjective but important.
*   **Versatile Applications:** The paper showcases the utility of LayouSyn in practical applications like LLM-based initialization and object insertion, demonstrating the potential of the method beyond pure layout generation. The integration with existing frameworks for image editing enhances its value.
*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly outlines the problem of reliance on proprietary LLMs and limited vocabulary, highlighting the gap LayouSyn aims to fill.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the system integrates several components in a novel way, the individual components (diffusion transformers, open-source LLMs) are not groundbreaking innovations in isolation. The novelty primarily lies in the specific combination and adaptation to the scene layout generation task.
*   **Limited Abation Depth:** While the model has an ablation study, the scope of this study is quite minimal. It could be a much stronger paper with a more rigorous and comprehensive ablation strategy.
*   **Dependency on LLMs:** Even though it uses lightweight open-source LLMs, the pipeline still depends on the quality of noun-phrase extraction and counting provided by the LLM. Errors or limitations in LLM capabilities could propagate through the pipeline. Further discussion and analysis of this dependency would be beneficial.
*   **Qualitative Subjectivity:** Evaluation involves the visual plausibility of the scene layouts. Plausibility is inherently subjective, and better more robust evaluation methods could be leveraged.

**Significance:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by providing a practical and accessible solution for generating scene layouts from text. The combination of open-source models and a specialized diffusion Transformer architecture offers a viable alternative to approaches that depend on proprietary LLMs. The applications highlighted demonstrate the potential of LayouSyn to facilitate controllable image generation and editing. The released codebase and models have already been adopted by other practitioners.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper introduces a valuable, accessible, and well-performing system for text-to-layout generation. It demonstrates clear improvements over existing methods, particularly in spatial and numerical reasoning, and highlights the importance of an open-source approach. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental, and a more thorough ablation study could further strengthen the claims. While the results are strong and the applications relevant, the paper is a high-quality incremental improvement and does not represent a fundamental breakthrough that would warrant a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### SOAEsV2-7B/72B: Full-Pipeline Optimization for State-Owned Enterprise LLMs via Continual Pre-Training, Domain-Progressive SFT and Distillation-Enhanced Speculative Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04723v1)
- **Authors**: Jingyang Deng, Ran Chen, Jo-Ku Cheng, Jinwen Ma
- **Abstract**: This study addresses key challenges in developing domain-specific large language models (LLMs) for Chinese state-owned assets and enterprises (SOAEs), where current approaches face three limitations: 1) constrained model capacity that limits knowledge integration and cross-task adaptability; 2) excessive reliance on domain-specific supervised fine-tuning (SFT) data, which neglects the broader applicability of general language patterns; and 3) inefficient inference acceleration for large models processing long contexts. In this work, we propose SOAEsV2-7B/72B, a specialized LLM series developed via a three-phase framework: 1) continual pre-training integrates domain knowledge while retaining base capabilities; 2) domain-progressive SFT employs curriculum-based learning strategy, transitioning from weakly relevant conversational data to expert-annotated SOAEs datasets to optimize domain-specific tasks; 3) distillation-enhanced speculative decoding accelerates inference via logit distillation between 72B target and 7B draft models, achieving 1.39-1.52$\times$ speedup without quality loss. Experimental results demonstrate that our domain-specific pre-training phase maintains 99.8% of original general language capabilities while significantly improving domain performance, resulting in a 1.08$\times$ improvement in Rouge-1 score and a 1.17$\times$ enhancement in BLEU-4 score. Ablation studies further show that domain-progressive SFT outperforms single-stage training, achieving 1.02$\times$ improvement in Rouge-1 and 1.06$\times$ in BLEU-4. Our work introduces a comprehensive, full-pipeline approach for optimizing SOAEs LLMs, bridging the gap between general language capabilities and domain-specific expertise.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SOAESV2-7B/72B, a specialized series of Large Language Models (LLMs) tailored for the Chinese State-Owned Assets and Enterprises (SOAEs) domain. The work addresses three key limitations of existing approaches: constrained model capacity, excessive reliance on domain-specific supervised fine-tuning (SFT) data neglecting broader language patterns, and inefficient inference acceleration for large models. The authors propose a three-phase framework: 1) continual pre-training to integrate domain knowledge while retaining general capabilities; 2) domain-progressive SFT employing a curriculum-based learning strategy that gradually transitions from weakly to strongly relevant data; and 3) distillation-enhanced speculative decoding to accelerate inference using a 7B draft model assisting a 72B target model. The experiments demonstrate that the approach maintains general language capabilities while improving domain performance, achieving speedups in inference without sacrificing quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a comprehensive, full-pipeline optimization approach for SOAEs LLMs, which marks a significant advancement over prior works that primarily focus on data curation, domain adaptation using smaller models (7B), and single-stage fine-tuning. While continual pre-training and speculative decoding are not entirely novel concepts in the general LLM field, their combined application with a domain-progressive SFT specifically designed for the SOAEs context *is* a novel contribution. The curriculum-driven approach within the SFT phase, prioritizing data relevance alignment over difficulty, is also a notable innovation.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work lies in its potential impact on the practical deployment of LLMs in the SOAEs sector. By addressing the key challenges of domain specificity, knowledge integration, and inference efficiency, the authors have developed a solution that better bridges the gap between general language capabilities and domain-specific expertise. The increased model size (72B) compared to previous SOAEs-specific models is a plus. The reported improvements in ROUGE and BLEU scores, alongside the speedup in inference, further validate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. It is useful for those doing SOAEs-specific NLP research, particularly where those in need of a large model and speedups in inference.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper tackles the problem holistically, addressing pre-training, fine-tuning, and inference optimization.
*   **Practical Relevance:** The research is motivated by real-world challenges faced in deploying LLMs in a specific industrial domain.
*   **Clearly Defined Methodology:** The paper provides a clear and detailed description of the proposed framework and its individual components.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The authors present thorough experimental results, demonstrating the effectiveness of each component and the overall approach.
*   **Ablation Studies:** They conduct ablation studies to analyze the contribution of each training strategy (CPT and progressive fine-tuning).
*   **Model scaling:** The model scales up to 72B, which contributes to knowledge capacity and general capabilities.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability:** The SOAEs domain is highly specific, raising questions about the generalizability of the proposed techniques to other domains.  While the authors suggest applicability to finance and healthcare, more evidence would strengthen this claim.
*   **Data Specificity:** The performance is heavily reliant on the quality and relevance of the curated domain-specific data. Replicating these results in other domains may require significant data engineering efforts.
*   **Baseline Limitations**: The baseline is a general Qwen model, it may be useful to compare against a strong fine-tuned version.
*   **No statistical significance tests:** Results are given, but not statistically significant.

**Justification for the Score:**

Despite some limitations, this paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of domain-specific LLM development. The comprehensive approach, practical relevance, and experimental validation justify a positive assessment. The combination of existing techniques in a novel way, tailored to a specific and important industrial domain, warrants a solid score. The score reflects the paper's incremental advancements and the impact that the framework can have on LLM deployment. The limitations related to generalizability and data specificity temper the score, as they highlight the need for further research.

### QBD-RankedDataGen: Generating Custom Ranked Datasets for Improving Query-By-Document Search Using LLM-Reranking with Reduced Human Effort
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04732v1)
- **Authors**: Sriram Gopalakrishnan, Sunandita Patra
- **Abstract**: The Query-By-Document (QBD) problem is an information retrieval problem where the query is a document, and the retrieved candidates are documents that match the query document, often in a domain or query specific manner. This can be crucial for tasks such as patent matching, legal or compliance case retrieval, and academic literature review. Existing retrieval methods, including keyword search and document embeddings, can be optimized with domain-specific datasets to improve QBD search performance. However, creating these domain-specific datasets is often costly and time-consuming. Our work introduces a process to generate custom QBD-search datasets and compares a set of methods to use in this problem, which we refer to as QBD-RankedDatagen. We provide a comparative analysis of our proposed methods in terms of cost, speed, and the human interface with the domain experts. The methods we compare leverage Large Language Models (LLMs) which can incorporate domain expert input to produce document scores and rankings, as well as explanations for human review. The process and methods for it that we present can significantly reduce human effort in dataset creation for custom domains while still obtaining sufficient expert knowledge for tuning retrieval models. We evaluate our methods on QBD datasets from the Text Retrieval Conference (TREC) and finetune the parameters of the BM25 model -- which is used in many industrial-strength search engines like OpenSearch -- using the generated data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "QBD-RANKEDDATAGEN," a process for generating custom-ranked datasets for query-by-document (QBD) search. It addresses the challenge of creating domain-specific QBD datasets, which are often costly and time-consuming. The authors propose using Large Language Models (LLMs) to re-rank retrieved document candidates, incorporating domain expert input to produce document scores, rankings, and explanations for human review. The goal is to reduce human effort in dataset creation while maintaining expert knowledge for tuning retrieval models. The paper compares different LLM-based re-ranking methods (pointwise and pairwise) on TREC datasets, evaluates BM25 performance after fine-tuning with the generated data, and discusses the human cost trade-offs of each method. The main contributions are a framework for generating ranked QBD datasets that reduces human effort, comparative analysis of re-ranking methods, and evaluation of BM25 fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic approach to QBD dataset generation using LLMs, specifically focusing on the re-ranking step and its interplay with human expert feedback. While LLM re-ranking itself is not new, applying it to the problem of creating custom-ranked QBD datasets, especially for long documents, and explicitly addressing the challenge of reducing human annotation effort, is a valuable contribution. The comparison of different LLM re-ranking methods (pointwise and pairwise) is also a useful practical contribution.  A potential weakness is the incremental nature of building upon existing LLM re-ranking techniques without introducing a groundbreaking algorithmic advancement.  The lack of a novel LLM method designed specifically for QBD limits the "novelty" score.

* **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the practical need for domain-specific QBD datasets, particularly in fields like law and patent matching, where expert knowledge is critical and data availability is limited. By demonstrating a process that reduces human effort while preserving expert input, the paper has the potential to make QBD model development more accessible and cost-effective. This could lead to improved retrieval performance in various real-world applications. The focus on long documents is also important, as existing research often focuses on shorter text. However, the experiments showed mixed results; the fact that BM25 tuning often didn't improve performance without human validation is a critical limitation.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Practical Problem:** Addresses a real-world need for domain-specific QBD datasets.
    * **Human-Centered Approach:** Focuses on reducing human effort in dataset creation.
    * **Systematic Evaluation:** Compares different LLM re-ranking methods and their impact on BM25 fine-tuning.
    * **Focus on Long Documents:** Addresses a gap in existing QBD research.
    * **Reproducibility:** Well-defined methodology that is easy to reproduce

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Novelty:** Builds on existing LLM re-ranking techniques without a fundamental breakthrough.
    * **Limited Experimental Results:** The experimental results for instruction based reranking and BM25 fine-tuning showed mixed or negative results (when compared to human ground truth); this weakens the overall significance and practical impact.
    * **Dataset Limitations:** Used existing datasets, could have benefitted from the creation of a new domain specific dataset.
    * **LLM Cost trade-off:** The impact of human intervention and associated cost trade-offs not fully explored.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach could influence future research on QBD dataset generation, particularly in areas where human expert knowledge is crucial. It could also inspire the development of more efficient LLM-based re-ranking methods specifically tailored for QBD tasks. The finding that human validation of results is necessary is also important for the field.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:** While the paper tackles a relevant and practical problem with a systematic approach leveraging LLMs, the novelty is somewhat incremental. The lack of performance gain, and a few cases of performance reduction for instruction based methods, also limits the practical impact. The work would have benefited from a more novel LLM-based technique tailored explicitly to the QBD problem, especially with respect to managing long contexts and human feedback, and from stronger empirical results. Nevertheless, the well-defined framework, clear methodology, explicit attention to human factors and well-articulated potential significance justify a solid score above average. The experimental results showed that human validation of the re-ranked results is often necessary, which somewhat diminishes the "reduced human effort" objective.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### The Promise and Limits of LLMs in Constructing Proofs and Hints for Logic Problems in Intelligent Tutoring Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04736v1)
- **Authors**: Sutapa Dey Tithi, Arun Kumar Ramesh, Clara DiMarco, Xiaoyi Tian, Nazia Alam, Kimia Fazeli, Tiffany Barnes
- **Abstract**: Intelligent tutoring systems have demonstrated effectiveness in teaching formal propositional logic proofs, but their reliance on template-based explanations limits their ability to provide personalized student feedback. While large language models (LLMs) offer promising capabilities for dynamic feedback generation, they risk producing hallucinations or pedagogically unsound explanations. We evaluated the stepwise accuracy of LLMs in constructing multi-step symbolic logic proofs, comparing six prompting techniques across four state-of-the-art LLMs on 358 propositional logic problems. Results show that DeepSeek-V3 achieved superior performance with 84.4% accuracy on stepwise proof construction and excelled particularly in simpler rules. We further used the best-performing LLM to generate explanatory hints for 1,050 unique student problem-solving states from a logic ITS and evaluated them on 4 criteria with both an LLM grader and human expert ratings on a 20% sample. Our analysis finds that LLM-generated hints were 75% accurate and rated highly by human evaluators on consistency and clarity, but did not perform as well explaining why the hint was provided or its larger context. Our results demonstrate that LLMs may be used to augment tutoring systems with logic tutoring hints, but requires additional modifications to ensure accuracy and pedagogical appropriateness.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper "The Promise and Limits of LLMs in Constructing Proofs and Hints for Logic Problems in Intelligent Tutoring Systems" investigates the effectiveness of large language models (LLMs) in generating step-by-step solutions and explanatory hints for propositional logic problems within an intelligent tutoring system (ITS) context. The authors compare six prompting techniques across four LLMs on a set of propositional logic problems. They then evaluate the hints generated by the best-performing LLM, DeepSeek-V3, using both an LLM grader and human experts, focusing on correctness, consistency, clarity, justification, and subgoaling. The results indicate that LLMs can achieve high accuracy in constructing proofs and generating hints. However, they face challenges in more complex rules, justification, and subgoaling. The paper concludes that LLMs hold promise for augmenting ITS with logic tutoring hints, but require careful modification to ensure accuracy and pedagogical appropriateness.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Relevant and Timely Topic:** The paper tackles a very relevant problem: integrating LLMs into ITSs to enhance personalized feedback. Given the current rise of LLMs, understanding their capabilities and limitations in educational contexts is crucial.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study employs a multi-faceted evaluation approach, combining automated logic checking, LLM grading, and human expert evaluation. This provides a more robust and nuanced understanding of LLM performance.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The authors use multiple prompts with varying complexity to find an optimized method for providing hints in an ITS. The prompt techniques tested show a thorough analysis and effort put towards optimization.
    *   **Focus on Pedagogical Quality:** Unlike many studies that focus solely on accuracy, the paper evaluates LLM-generated content based on pedagogical principles (consistency, clarity, justification, subgoaling), which is critical for effective learning.
    *   **Clear Results and Discussion:** The results are presented clearly, and the discussion highlights both the strengths and weaknesses of LLMs in this context, acknowledging the challenges in ensuring complete accuracy and pedagogical soundness.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Logical Rules:** The study focuses specifically on propositional logic, which may limit the generalizability of the findings to other domains within formal logic or other subject areas.
    *   **Data Set Limitations:** The reliance on a specific data set and interactions within the developed Logic Tutor might introduce some bias. While the interactions were real, a more varied student population would strengthen the study.
    *   **Sample Size in Expert Evaluation:** The 20% sample size used for human expert evaluation might not fully capture the nuances of LLM-generated hints. While they did have 2 experts reviewing, the sample size could be a potential point of critique.
    *   **LLM Grading Limitations:** The paper mentions that the Rubric-based LLM grader did not have agreement with human graders, which raises concerns about using LLM graders in this context.
    *   **Limited Evaluation of LLM-constructed full solutions:** The paper mentions limitations to single-step accuracy, but the limitation of not evaluating the full solutions could be a detractor to their research.
*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper's main contribution lies in its focus on the integration of LLMs into Intelligent Tutoring Systems in formal logic. While other works might have explored LLMs' capabilities in solving logic problems, it investigates LLMs’ capabilities in multi-step symbolic logic proof construction.
    *   The methodology is rigorous, and the results show a clear potential for LLMs to be integrated into tutoring systems for formal logic, but requires more work to achieve an optimal output.

**Justification**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by evaluating LLMs' ability to generate appropriate and well-contextualized hints in an ITS for formal logic. The novelty lies in focusing on pedagogical soundness *within* an ITS environment, not just solving the problem. The limitations discussed are acknowledged and the study is well-done, but still not without its limitations. The future implications and acknowledgement of limitations gives the paper a good foundation for future research.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Hyb-KAN ViT: Hybrid Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks Augmented Vision Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04740v1)
- **Authors**: Sainath Dey, Mitul Goswami, Jashika Sethi, Prasant Kumar Pattnaik
- **Abstract**: This study addresses the inherent limitations of Multi-Layer Perceptrons (MLPs) in Vision Transformers (ViTs) by introducing Hybrid Kolmogorov-Arnold Network (KAN)-ViT (Hyb-KAN ViT), a novel framework that integrates wavelet-based spectral decomposition and spline-optimized activation functions, prior work has failed to focus on the prebuilt modularity of the ViT architecture and integration of edge detection capabilities of Wavelet functions. We propose two key modules: Efficient-KAN (Eff-KAN), which replaces MLP layers with spline functions and Wavelet-KAN (Wav-KAN), leveraging orthogonal wavelet transforms for multi-resolution feature extraction. These modules are systematically integrated in ViT encoder layers and classification heads to enhance spatial-frequency modeling while mitigating computational bottlenecks. Experiments on ImageNet-1K (Image Recognition), COCO (Object Detection and Instance Segmentation), and ADE20K (Semantic Segmentation) demonstrate state-of-the-art performance with Hyb-KAN ViT. Ablation studies validate the efficacy of wavelet-driven spectral priors in segmentation and spline-based efficiency in detection tasks. The framework establishes a new paradigm for balancing parameter efficiency and multi-scale representation in vision architectures.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Hyb-KAN ViT paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Hyb-KAN ViT, a novel Vision Transformer architecture that replaces traditional Multi-Layer Perceptrons (MLPs) with Hybrid Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs).  Hyb-KAN ViT incorporates two key modules: Efficient-KAN (Eff-KAN), using spline-optimized activation functions, and Wavelet-KAN (Wav-KAN), leveraging orthogonal wavelet transforms for multi-resolution feature extraction.  The authors systematically integrate these modules into ViT encoder layers and classification heads to enhance spatial-frequency modeling and mitigate computational bottlenecks. Experiments across ImageNet-1K, COCO, and ADE20K datasets demonstrate state-of-the-art performance. Ablation studies highlight the efficacy of wavelet-driven spectral priors in segmentation and spline-based efficiency in detection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the hybrid integration of spline-based KANs and wavelet-based KANs *within* a ViT architecture. While both KANs and wavelet transforms have been explored separately in other contexts, their combination in the ViT framework to address MLP limitations is a unique contribution. The strategic deployment of Eff-KAN and Wav-KAN in specific layers (encoder vs. classification head) adds another layer of architectural novelty. While the core concepts (KANs, wavelets, transformers) are not novel individually, their synergistic application to vision transformers represents a significant advancement.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its potential to address the computational limitations of MLPs in ViTs while improving accuracy. The experimental results show state-of-the-art performance on standard benchmark datasets, suggesting the effectiveness of the proposed architecture. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contributions of each module (Eff-KAN and Wav-KAN) and their impact on different vision tasks. Furthermore, demonstrating the trade-offs in parameter efficiency, computational cost, and accuracy across model variants is beneficial for the research community.
    The modular design offers flexibility for exploring different configurations and provides a robust approach for visual recognition tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong experimental results on multiple benchmark datasets.
    *   Thorough ablation studies that validate the effectiveness of each module.
    *   Clear explanation of the architectural design and the motivation behind each component.
    *   Addresses a significant limitation of ViTs (MLP bottlenecks).
    *   Well-written and clearly presents the proposed method and its benefits.
    *   Introduces several distinct wavelet families and tests their benefits across a variety of large image sets.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the results are state-of-the-art, the performance gains may not be substantial enough to warrant widespread adoption in all scenarios. The incremental improvement needs to be considered in light of the increased complexity.
    *   The computational complexity remains a concern. Although the authors mitigate the bottlenecks, KANs still introduce overhead compared to MLPs. The parameter count for Hyb-KAN ViT-B is still larger and require more computations, limiting its usefulness.
    *   Further analysis could investigate the reasons why Wav-KAN dominates in semantic segmentation and hybrids in object detection. A more comprehensive explanation could add another level of contributions.
    *   Limited analysis of edge cases and potential failure modes of the model could be provided.
    *   Reproducibility could be improved by providing more detailed instructions on how to run the code.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in Vision Transformers by:
    *   Encouraging the exploration of alternative building blocks beyond MLPs.
    *   Highlighting the importance of spectral-spatial priors in ViT design.
    *   Inspiring the development of more efficient KAN implementations.
    *   Providing a framework for integrating wavelet transforms into deep learning architectures.
    *   It opens the door for more adaptive vision research directions.

*   **Score:** 7.5

    **Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-executed architectural modification to ViTs, addressing a critical limitation and achieving state-of-the-art results. The thorough ablation studies and detailed analysis of performance trade-offs strengthen the contribution. While the computational complexity remains a concern and further investigations could enhance the explanation of model behavior, the paper represents a significant advancement in the field and has the potential to influence future research in Vision Transformers. The work provides a solid foundation for further exploration and optimization of KAN-based architectures for vision tasks. It's a high-quality contribution, justifying a score above average but falling short of being truly groundbreaking due to existing computational limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Proposal for Evaluating the Operational Risk for ChatBots based on Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04784v1)
- **Authors**: Pedro Pinacho-Davidson, Fernando Gutierrez, Pablo Zapata, Rodolfo Vergara, Pablo Aqueveque
- **Abstract**: The emergence of Generative AI (Gen AI) and Large Language Models (LLMs) has enabled more advanced chatbots capable of human-like interactions. However, these conversational agents introduce a broader set of operational risks that extend beyond traditional cybersecurity considerations. In this work, we propose a novel, instrumented risk-assessment metric that simultaneously evaluates potential threats to three key stakeholders: the service-providing organization, end users, and third parties. Our approach incorporates the technical complexity required to induce erroneous behaviors in the chatbot--ranging from non-induced failures to advanced prompt-injection attacks--as well as contextual factors such as the target industry, user age range, and vulnerability severity. To validate our metric, we leverage Garak, an open-source framework for LLM vulnerability testing. We further enhance Garak to capture a variety of threat vectors (e.g., misinformation, code hallucinations, social engineering, and malicious code generation). Our methodology is demonstrated in a scenario involving chatbots that employ retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), showing how the aggregated risk scores guide both short-term mitigation and longer-term improvements in model design and deployment. The results underscore the importance of multi-dimensional risk assessments in operationalizing secure, reliable AI-driven conversational systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel, multi-dimensional risk assessment metric for chatbots based on large language models (LLMs). It addresses the limitations of traditional cybersecurity assessments by incorporating risks not only to the system providing the service but also to end-users and third parties, considering the technical complexity required to exploit vulnerabilities, the target industry's sensitivity, and user age demographics.  The metric is implemented using Garak, an open-source LLM vulnerability testing framework, enhanced with new probes to capture a wider range of threat vectors. The methodology is demonstrated in a case study of a chatbot for university rules and regulations, highlighting the impact of the proposed metric on risk assessment and mitigation strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the comprehensive nature of its risk assessment metric. Traditional cybersecurity focuses mainly on the direct damage to the service provider. This paper, however, explicitly incorporates the risks to end-users and third parties into a quantifiable metric. The consideration of context-specific factors such as industry and user demographics also adds to the novelty. Using a well known open source tool for the metric also allows easy adoption and further development.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a crucial gap in the security assessment of LLM-based applications.  LLMs introduce new risks beyond traditional cybersecurity, particularly those related to misinformation, social engineering, and harmful content generation. By providing a structured, repeatable, and context-aware methodology, the paper contributes to a more responsible and secure deployment of LLM-powered chatbots. The use of Garak as a platform also helps bring the tool and the paper's methodology to a wider cybersecurity audience.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Risk Model:** The framework holistically considers risks to the service provider, users, and third parties.
    *   **Contextual Factors:** Industry-specific and user-demographic considerations enhance the practicality and relevance of the metric.
    *   **Open-Source Implementation:** The Garak-based implementation enables reproducibility, extensibility, and adoption by the broader community.
    *   **Case Study:** The university chatbot case study demonstrates the applicability and value of the proposed metric.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** The case study, while illustrative, could be expanded with more comprehensive data across a variety of real-world chatbot implementations. This would strengthen the claim that the proposed metric is valid.
    *   **Subjectivity in Weighting Factors:** The assignment of weights and multipliers for industry sectors and user demographics may involve some degree of subjectivity. While the paper references relevant studies, further justification or sensitivity analyses could improve the robustness of these parameters.
    *   **Dependency on Garak:** While using Garak is beneficial for accessibility, it also couples the metric to the framework's limitations and updates. A more generic formulation could increase the long-term applicability.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Technical Aspects:** The use of prompt injection for vulnerability testing, while relevant, is not a novel technique in itself. The paper could benefit from more discussion of how the metric can be used to guide specific countermeasures for each of the attack types mentioned in the vulnerabilities and metrics.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence how LLM-based applications are assessed for security and safety. By promoting a broader perspective on risk and offering a practical implementation, it could encourage developers and organizations to adopt more responsible AI deployment practices. Furthermore, it provides a useful foundation for building similar risk assessment tools that consider human-centric factors.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper presents a solid and relevant contribution to the field of LLM security with a novel risk assessment metric. Its strengths lie in the comprehensive risk model, the consideration of contextual factors, and the open-source implementation. However, the limited empirical validation and potential subjectivity in the weighting factors, as well as the limited innovation in technical implementations slightly detract from its overall impact. Despite these limitations, the paper addresses a key gap in LLM security assessment and provides a practical framework for improving the safety and reliability of chatbot applications.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Safeguard-by-Development: A Privacy-Enhanced Development Paradigm for Multi-Agent Collaboration Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04799v1)
- **Authors**: Jian Cui, Zichuan Li, Luyi Xing, Xiaojing Liao
- **Abstract**: Multi-agent collaboration systems (MACS), powered by large language models (LLMs), solve complex problems efficiently by leveraging each agent's specialization and communication between agents. However, the inherent exchange of information between agents and their interaction with external environments, such as LLM, tools, and users, inevitably introduces significant risks of sensitive data leakage, including vulnerabilities to attacks like prompt injection and reconnaissance. Existing MACS fail to enable privacy controls, making it challenging to manage sensitive information securely. In this paper, we take the first step to address the MACS's data leakage threat at the system development level through a privacy-enhanced development paradigm, Maris. Maris enables rigorous message flow control within MACS by embedding reference monitors into key multi-agent conversation components. We implemented Maris as an integral part of AutoGen, a widely adopted open-source multi-agent development framework. Then, we evaluate Maris for its effectiveness and performance overhead on privacy-critical MACS use cases, including healthcare, supply chain optimization, and personalized recommendation system. The result shows that Maris achieves satisfactory effectiveness, performance overhead and practicability for adoption.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the data leakage problem in multi-agent collaboration systems (MACS) powered by large language models (LLMs). It introduces Maris, a privacy-enhanced development paradigm designed to mitigate sensitive data disclosure risks at the system development level. Maris embeds reference monitors into key multi-agent conversation components to control message flow and enforces privacy policies through a Data Safeguard Engine. The authors implemented Maris as part of the AutoGen framework and evaluated its effectiveness and performance overhead in several privacy-critical use cases, demonstrating a balance between privacy protection and system usability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary strength lies in its focus on a relatively unexplored area: data leakage *within* the multi-agent system itself, as opposed to just agent-environment interactions. While some prior work has looked at prompt injection and data exfiltration from LLMs in general, this paper takes a system-level view of multi-agent privacy. The concept of a data safeguard engine embedded in the development framework is a valuable contribution. However, the individual components (reference monitors, policy enforcement via LLMs) are not entirely new on their own. The novelty is in the integration and application to the MACS context.

* **Significance:** Data leakage in MACS is a growing concern, especially with increased use in sensitive domains like healthcare and finance. As the complexity of multi-agent system increases, the risks of data breaches also increase. By providing a systematic way to manage sensitive information at the development level, Maris offers a valuable tool for building more secure and compliant MACS. The paper addresses a practically relevant problem and offers a potentially impactful solution. The implementation within AutoGen helps facilitate easier adoption. However, the work is still preliminary and the generalizability of the framework to systems outside of AutoGen is not fully tested yet. The evaluation is reasonably thorough, but more real-world deployments and comparisons against other techniques would be necessary to fully establish the significance.

* **Strengths:**
    * Addresses an important and timely problem.
    * System-level approach focusing on a under-explored area.
    * Well-defined design goals and principles.
    * Empirical evaluation with real-world use cases.
    * Open-source implementation promotes reproducibility and adoption.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Reliance on a local LLM for policy enforcement introduces potential privacy and performance concerns. This reliance may become an issue as MACSs become more complex.
    * Generalizability to frameworks beyond AutoGen is not thoroughly demonstrated. The integration is deeply integrated within the AutoGen framework and its core classes.
    * Evaluation, while comprehensive, would benefit from more diversity in use cases and deployment scenarios to show generalizability.
    * There is a considerable performance overhead due to the extra data processing and added complexity.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more secure MACS by raising awareness of the data leakage problem and providing a practical solution. The open-source implementation can facilitate adoption and further research in this area. It could lead to the incorporation of similar privacy safeguards into other multi-agent development frameworks.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7. While the paper addresses an important problem with a practical solution, the novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary. The dependence on a local LLM for policy enforcement, the somewhat limited generalizability beyond AutoGen, and the performance overhead limit the overall impact. The evaluation shows promise, but further validation is needed.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Red Teaming the Mind of the Machine: A Systematic Evaluation of Prompt Injection and Jailbreak Vulnerabilities in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04806v1)
- **Authors**: Chetan Pathade
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly integrated into consumer and enterprise applications. Despite their capabilities, they remain susceptible to adversarial attacks such as prompt injection and jailbreaks that override alignment safeguards. This paper provides a systematic investigation of jailbreak strategies against various state-of-the-art LLMs. We categorize over 1,400 adversarial prompts, analyze their success against GPT-4, Claude 2, Mistral 7B, and Vicuna, and examine their generalizability and construction logic. We further propose layered mitigation strategies and recommend a hybrid red-teaming and sandboxing approach for robust LLM security.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Red Teaming the Mind of the Machine: A Systematic Evaluation of Prompt Injection and Jailbreak Vulnerabilities in LLMs" presents a comprehensive empirical study of prompt injection and jailbreak attacks against several state-of-the-art Large Language Models (LLMs), including GPT-4, Claude 2, Mistral 7B, and Vicuna. The authors collected a dataset of over 1,400 adversarial prompts, categorized them by attack vector, and evaluated their effectiveness across different models. The study analyzes the attack success rate, prompt generalizability, time-to-bypass, and failure modes.  It further examines scenario-specific vulnerabilities (e.g., political, legal, explicit content), prompt transferability, and the impact of prompt length and obfuscation. Finally, the paper proposes layered mitigation strategies and advocates for a hybrid red-teaming and sandboxing approach to enhance LLM security.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Empirical Analysis:** The paper provides a thorough and well-structured empirical evaluation of prompt injection attacks, utilizing a large dataset of adversarial prompts. The inclusion of both closed (GPT-4, Claude 2) and open-source LLMs (Mistral 7B, Vicuna) is valuable.
    *   **Detailed Taxonomy and Analysis:** The categorization of jailbreak prompts by attack vector, the analysis of failure modes, and the investigation of prompt transferability contribute to a deeper understanding of the vulnerabilities. The identification of scenario-specific vulnerabilities is insightful.
    *   **Practical Recommendations:** The paper offers practical mitigation strategies based on the empirical findings, including system prompt hardening, behavior-based anomaly detection, and input sanitization techniques.
    *   **Reproducibility Considerations:**  The authors made some effort toward reproducibility by freezing model versions and specifying the evaluation metrics and automation pipeline.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Mitigation Strategies:** The proposed mitigation strategies largely draw upon existing work (PromptShield, Palisade). While synthesizing and recommending a layered defense is useful, the individual components are not particularly novel.
    *   **Evaluation of Defenses:** The paper only tests the "defense coverage and bypass rate" with a limited "subset of successful jailbreaks". A more in-depth study of defense strategies and their limitations would be a great addition.
    *   **Limited Discussion on Ethical Implications:** While the paper briefly mentions ethical concerns, a more detailed discussion of the ethical implications of disclosing jailbreak prompts and the potential for misuse would strengthen the analysis.
    *   **Reproducibility challenges:** Despite mentioning reproducibility considerations, the details are insufficient for full replication of the experimental results, especially considering the rapidly evolving nature of LLMs and the proprietary nature of some of the models tested.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper significantly contributes to the growing body of research on LLM security. The systematic evaluation of prompt injection attacks and the identification of specific vulnerabilities provide valuable insights for developers and security practitioners.
    *   The comprehensive analysis of prompt transferability highlights the systemic nature of these vulnerabilities and the need for robust defense mechanisms.
    *   The paper raises important questions about the effectiveness of current safety alignment techniques and the need for continuous adversarial testing in LLM development pipelines.

*   **Novelty:** While the methodology and comprehensiveness are strong, the novelty of the findings is moderate. Other papers have explored prompt injection, but this one distinguishes itself through its systematic nature, large dataset, and attention to prompt transferability. However, the recommended mitigation strategies are less novel.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a solid understanding of the problem space and provides a useful empirical contribution to the field of LLM security. The systematic evaluation, detailed analysis, and practical recommendations add value. However, the lack of significant novelty in the proposed mitigation strategies and the limited discussion on ethical implications detract from its overall impact. The reproducibility considerations, though present, remain limited in scope, making it challenging to replicate the results fully.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Steerable Scene Generation with Post Training and Inference-Time Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04831v1)
- **Authors**: Nicholas Pfaff, Hongkai Dai, Sergey Zakharov, Shun Iwase, Russ Tedrake
- **Abstract**: Training robots in simulation requires diverse 3D scenes that reflect the specific challenges of downstream tasks. However, scenes that satisfy strict task requirements, such as high-clutter environments with plausible spatial arrangement, are rare and costly to curate manually. Instead, we generate large-scale scene data using procedural models that approximate realistic environments for robotic manipulation, and adapt it to task-specific goals. We do this by training a unified diffusion-based generative model that predicts which objects to place from a fixed asset library, along with their SE(3) poses. This model serves as a flexible scene prior that can be adapted using reinforcement learning-based post training, conditional generation, or inference-time search, steering generation toward downstream objectives even when they differ from the original data distribution. Our method enables goal-directed scene synthesis that respects physical feasibility and scales across scene types. We introduce a novel MCTS-based inference-time search strategy for diffusion models, enforce feasibility via projection and simulation, and release a dataset of over 44 million SE(3) scenes spanning five diverse environments. Website with videos, code, data, and model weights: https://steerable-scene-generation.github.io/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a method for generating steerable 3D scenes for robotics simulation. It trains a diffusion-based generative model on procedurally generated SE(3) scenes (object ID, 3D position and orientation). The key contribution is adapting this prior to task-specific goals using three techniques: reinforcement learning (RL) post-training, conditional generation (conditioning on text or partial scenes), and inference-time Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to optimize for objectives like physical feasibility and high clutter. The approach allows for goal-directed scene synthesis, scales across scene types, enforces physical feasibility through projection and simulation, and provides a dataset of over 44 million SE(3) scenes spanning five environments. The paper demonstrates the utility of the generated scenes by teleoperating a KUKA iiwa robot in a simulated scene.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper combines several existing techniques in a novel way. Diffusion models for scene generation are known, as are RL post-training and conditional generation. The primary novelty lies in (1) applying these techniques to SE(3) scenes instead of the more common SE(2) scenes with floor-aligned objects and (2) introducing an MCTS-based inference-time search strategy for diffusion models. The MCTS approach for guiding diffusion model inference is a relatively unexplored area and is a solid technical contribution. The emphasis on physical feasibility and robotic manipulation relevance is also a positive aspect compared to more visually oriented scene generation works.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical need in robotics: generating diverse and relevant training data for simulation. Manually creating complex, cluttered scenes is time-consuming and expensive. The ability to steer scene generation towards specific tasks, especially those requiring high clutter or physical interaction, is valuable. Releasing a dataset of 44 million scenes is a strong contribution, providing a valuable resource for the robotics community. The demonstration of teleoperation in generated scenes offers tangible evidence of the practicality of the approach for robotics applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive approach combining diffusion models, RL, conditional generation, and MCTS.
    *   Focus on physically realistic SE(3) scene generation.
    *   Large-scale dataset release.
    *   Demonstration of teleoperation in generated scenes.
    *   MCTS-based inference is a novel contribution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The novelty of individual components is limited; the overall approach is more of a synthesis of existing methods.
    *   While physical feasibility is addressed, the simulations are still operating within the limitations of the simulator. Sim-to-real transfer is not explicitly addressed, although the authors motivate their approach with the sim-to-real paradigm.
    *   The reliance on procedural generation for training data means that the initial dataset might not fully capture the complexity of real-world environments, which could potentially limit the realism and transferability of the generated scenes.
    *   The evaluation metrics are primarily image-based, which may not fully capture the quality of SE(3) scene generation for robotics applications. Task-based metrics (e.g., robot performance) would strengthen the evaluation.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical method and a large dataset for generating task-relevant 3D scenes for robotic simulation. The MCTS-based inference strategy is a promising direction for further research on steering generative models.

**Justification:**

While the paper doesn't introduce groundbreaking new algorithms, it presents a well-engineered system that addresses a practical need in robotics. The combination of techniques, the focus on physical realism, the large dataset, and the MCTS approach make it a valuable contribution. However, the reliance on procedural data and the lack of a sim-to-real transfer evaluation limits its impact somewhat.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models are Autonomous Cyber Defenders
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04843v1)
- **Authors**: Sebastián R. Castro, Roberto Campbell, Nancy Lau, Octavio Villalobos, Jiaqi Duan, Alvaro A. Cardenas
- **Abstract**: Fast and effective incident response is essential to prevent adversarial cyberattacks. Autonomous Cyber Defense (ACD) aims to automate incident response through Artificial Intelligence (AI) agents that plan and execute actions. Most ACD approaches focus on single-agent scenarios and leverage Reinforcement Learning (RL). However, ACD RL-trained agents depend on costly training, and their reasoning is not always explainable or transferable. Large Language Models (LLMs) can address these concerns by providing explainable actions in general security contexts. Researchers have explored LLM agents for ACD but have not evaluated them on multi-agent scenarios or interacting with other ACD agents. In this paper, we show the first study on how LLMs perform in multi-agent ACD environments by proposing a new integration to the CybORG CAGE 4 environment. We examine how ACD teams of LLM and RL agents can interact by proposing a novel communication protocol. Our results highlight the strengths and weaknesses of LLMs and RL and help us identify promising research directions to create, train, and deploy future teams of ACD agents.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the application of Large Language Models (LLMs) in Autonomous Cyber Defense (ACD) environments, specifically within the CybORG CAGE 4 multi-agent simulation. The authors develop a framework to integrate LLMs alongside Reinforcement Learning (RL) agents, introduce a novel communication protocol for these heterogeneous agents, and evaluate their performance against diverse adversaries. The study highlights the strengths and weaknesses of both LLMs and RL in the ACD context, suggesting potential research directions for creating more robust and explainable ACD agents. Key contributions include the LLM+RL ACD framework, a communication protocol for diverse ACD agents, and an evaluation of LLMs and RL agents, identification of weaknesses and identification of interesting future research directions, including model and prompt tuning and goal-based planning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its integration of LLMs into the CybORG CAGE 4 multi-agent ACD environment. While prior work has explored LLMs in ACD, this appears to be the first study focusing on multi-agent scenarios and their interaction with RL agents *within* the CybORG framework, and introducing a communication protocol. The communication protocol itself, while simple, is a practical contribution for enabling interaction between LLMs and RL agents in this context. The analysis of LLM reasoning in ACD using clustering techniques also adds a layer of novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to address limitations of traditional RL-based ACD approaches, such as explainability and transferability. By demonstrating the feasibility of LLMs in multi-agent ACD environments and highlighting their strengths (e.g., reasoning and potential for generalization), the authors open new avenues for research.  The identification of key areas for improvement (prompt tuning, addressing hallucinations, environment compatibility) provides a valuable roadmap for future work. However, the paper is an initial exploration, and the experimental results, while informative, don't definitively prove the superiority of LLMs over RL in ACD. The focus on lighter models (GPT-4o-mini and similar) for speed, while understandable, limits the scope of the analysis because it sacrifices some performance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly defined research questions and contributions.
    *   Well-structured methodology with a focus on practical implementation and evaluation.
    *   Introduction of a novel LLM+RL ACD framework and communication protocol.
    *   Identification of LLM strengths and weaknesses in the ACD context.
    *   Thorough discussion of potential research directions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited experimental validation; the results don't show significant benefits over RL and use limited and simple scenarios. The performance differences between the LLM-driven and RL-driven agents could be due to the suboptimal prompt tuning.
    *   The communication protocol, while practical, is relatively basic. More sophisticated protocols might be necessary for more complex scenarios.
    *   The reliance on specific models (GPT-4o-mini) for the LLM part of the experiment restricts the generalizability of conclusions.
    *   Limited discussion of the security implications of deploying LLMs in ACD, considering potential vulnerabilities to adversarial attacks.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research by encouraging the exploration of LLMs as components in ACD systems. The LLM+RL ACD framework and communication protocol can serve as a foundation for building more advanced and explainable ACD solutions.  However, further research is needed to address the identified limitations and validate the long-term effectiveness of LLMs in real-world scenarios.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper presents a valuable, well-reasoned, and relatively novel contribution to the field of ACD. However, it is more of a promising start with the identification of limitations and interesting future works than a fully realized solution. The experimental results are relatively limited, and the practical implementation of the system relies on more lightweight and older LLM models. Further work is needed in prompt tuning, more sophisticated scenarios, and deployment considerations. The simplicity of the communication protocol is another limitation.
Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Osiris: A Lightweight Open-Source Hallucination Detection System
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04844v1)
- **Authors**: Alex Shan, John Bauer, Christopher D. Manning
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems have gained widespread adoption by application builders because they leverage sources of truth to enable Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate more factually sound responses. However, hallucinations, instances of LLM responses that are unfaithful to the provided context, often prevent these systems from being deployed in production environments. Current hallucination detection methods typically involve human evaluation or the use of closed-source models to review RAG system outputs for hallucinations. Both human evaluators and closed-source models suffer from scaling issues due to their high costs and slow inference speeds. In this work, we introduce a perturbed multi-hop QA dataset with induced hallucinations. Via supervised fine-tuning on our dataset, we achieve better recall with a 7B model than GPT-4o on the RAGTruth hallucination detection benchmark and offer competitive performance on precision and accuracy, all while using a fraction of the parameters. Code is released at our repository.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Osiris: A Lightweight Open-Source Hallucination Detection System":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Osiris-7B, a lightweight, open-source model for detecting hallucinations in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. The authors address the limitations of existing hallucination detection methods, which often rely on expensive, closed-source models or human evaluation, both suffering from scalability issues. Osiris-7B is fine-tuned on a novel, perturbed multi-hop question-answering dataset (derived from MuSiQue) specifically designed to induce and detect hallucinations.  The paper claims that Osiris-7B achieves higher recall than GPT-40 on the RAGTruth benchmark while maintaining competitive precision and F1 scores, and offers faster inference speeds. The code is released to facilitate further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a critical problem:** Hallucination detection is a major bottleneck in deploying RAG systems in real-world applications.
    *   **Open-source and lightweight:** Providing an open-source alternative to closed-source models like GPT-40 addresses a significant need for accessibility and reproducibility.  The lightweight nature of the model allows for faster inference and lower computational costs, making it more practical for deployment.
    *   **Novel dataset:** The creation of a perturbed multi-hop QA dataset is a valuable contribution. The focus on multi-hop reasoning is important, as it reflects the complexity of real-world RAG applications where hallucinations are most challenging to detect. Perturbing both positive and negative samples in the dataset to fine-tune for detecting hallucinations is also a novel idea.
    *   **Empirical results:** The paper presents empirical results demonstrating improved recall compared to GPT-40 on the RAGTruth benchmark. The results are further strengthened by presenting the improvements over the base models obtained after finetuning on the proposed dataset.
    *   **Clear problem definition and methodology:** The paper clearly defines the problem, explains the methodology, and presents the results in a structured manner.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Evaluation:** Evaluation primarily focuses on the RAGTruth benchmark. While valuable, this single benchmark might not fully represent the diversity of RAG applications and hallucination types.  A more comprehensive evaluation across multiple datasets or real-world use cases would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Dataset Construction Reliance on GPT-40:** The dataset construction process relies on GPT-40 for verification and hallucination induction. This raises concerns about potential biases introduced by GPT-40 and limits the diversity of the induced hallucinations.  The paper acknowledges this, but further investigation into the impact of this reliance is warranted.
    *   **Precision vs. Recall Trade-off:** While the paper emphasizes the importance of high recall, the impact of sacrificing precision is not thoroughly discussed. In some applications, a high false positive rate (flagging correct responses as hallucinations) can be equally detrimental to a high false negative rate. A deeper analysis of the trade-off between precision and recall in different application contexts would be beneficial.
    *   **Generalization:** While the dataset is designed to reduce shortcut-driven reasoning, the degree to which the model generalizes to completely unseen knowledge domains and RAG systems remains unclear.
    *   **Limited comparison with existing methods:** There's a limited discussion and comparison with other open-source hallucination detection approaches. While GPT-40 is used as a baseline, a more thorough comparison with a broader range of relevant techniques would provide a clearer understanding of Osiris-7B's relative strengths and weaknesses.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The primary novelty lies in the combination of: 1) a lightweight, open-source hallucination detection model, and 2) a novel perturbed multi-hop QA dataset for fine-tuning. While the individual components are not entirely groundbreaking, their combination addresses a practical need for accessible and efficient hallucination detection in RAG systems. The improved recall compared to GPT-40 is also significant, suggesting that domain-specific fine-tuning can yield better results than relying solely on large, general-purpose models. The open-source nature and fast inference speed certainly contributes to its value, since it will lower the barrier to entry for researchers.

    The significance is moderate. It provides a practical tool for the community, and the dataset is a valuable resource. However, the limitations in evaluation and the reliance on GPT-40 temper the overall impact.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

Osiris-7B makes a worthwhile contribution to the field by providing an open-source and lightweight alternative for hallucination detection. The novel dataset and the demonstrated improvement in recall compared to GPT-40 are positive signs. However, the limitations in evaluation, the reliance on GPT-40 for dataset construction, and the lack of a comprehensive comparison with other approaches prevent it from achieving a higher score.  The potential for bias introduced by GPT-40, the limited generalizability, and the unaddressed precision vs. recall tradeoff need to be carefully considered. The true impact will depend on how well Osiris-7B performs in diverse real-world RAG applications and whether it inspires further research in open-source hallucination detection methods.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### HiPerRAG: High-Performance Retrieval Augmented Generation for Scientific Insights
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04846v1)
- **Authors**: Ozan Gokdemir, Carlo Siebenschuh, Alexander Brace, Azton Wells, Brian Hsu, Kyle Hippe, Priyanka V. Setty, Aswathy Ajith, J. Gregory Pauloski, Varuni Sastry, Sam Foreman, Huihuo Zheng, Heng Ma, Bharat Kale, Nicholas Chia, Thomas Gibbs, Michael E. Papka, Thomas Brettin, Francis J. Alexander, Anima Anandkumar, Ian Foster, Rick Stevens, Venkatram Vishwanath, Arvind Ramanathan
- **Abstract**: The volume of scientific literature is growing exponentially, leading to underutilized discoveries, duplicated efforts, and limited cross-disciplinary collaboration. Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) offers a way to assist scientists by improving the factuality of Large Language Models (LLMs) in processing this influx of information. However, scaling RAG to handle millions of articles introduces significant challenges, including the high computational costs associated with parsing documents and embedding scientific knowledge, as well as the algorithmic complexity of aligning these representations with the nuanced semantics of scientific content. To address these issues, we introduce HiPerRAG, a RAG workflow powered by high performance computing (HPC) to index and retrieve knowledge from more than 3.6 million scientific articles. At its core are Oreo, a high-throughput model for multimodal document parsing, and ColTrast, a query-aware encoder fine-tuning algorithm that enhances retrieval accuracy by using contrastive learning and late-interaction techniques. HiPerRAG delivers robust performance on existing scientific question answering benchmarks and two new benchmarks introduced in this work, achieving 90% accuracy on SciQ and 76% on PubMedQA-outperforming both domain-specific models like PubMedGPT and commercial LLMs such as GPT-4. Scaling to thousands of GPUs on the Polaris, Sunspot, and Frontier supercomputers, HiPerRAG delivers million document-scale RAG workflows for unifying scientific knowledge and fostering interdisciplinary innovation.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary and a rigorous critical evaluation of the paper "HiPerRAG: High-Performance Retrieval Augmented Generation for Scientific Insights," including assigning a novelty and significance score with a thorough justification.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HiPerRAG, a high-performance computing (HPC) powered Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) workflow designed to index and retrieve knowledge from a large corpus of scientific articles (over 3.6 million). It addresses the challenges of scaling RAG to handle the exponential growth of scientific literature by focusing on efficient document parsing and accurate retrieval. The key components are: 1) Oreo, a high-throughput multimodal document parsing model; and 2) ColTrast, a query-aware encoder fine-tuning algorithm using contrastive learning and late interaction for enhanced retrieval accuracy. HiPerRAG demonstrates robust performance on scientific question answering benchmarks, achieving high accuracy on SciQ and PubMedQA, outperforming domain-specific models and commercial LLMs like GPT-4. The system is scaled on Polaris, Sunspot, and Frontier supercomputers to deliver million-document-scale RAG workflows, fostering interdisciplinary innovation.  The authors also introduce two new biomedical Q/A benchmarks for RAG.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*Strengths:*

*   **Addressing a Significant Problem:** The paper tackles a crucial challenge: the overwhelming volume of scientific literature and the need for effective knowledge access and synthesis. The use of RAG is a natural and well-motivated approach to improve the factuality of LLM outputs in this domain.
*   **Novel Components:** The introduction of Oreo and ColTrast presents potentially significant advancements. Oreo seems to offer a practical and efficient solution for parsing complex scientific documents, balancing speed and accuracy. ColTrast's combination of contrastive learning and late interaction is a well-reasoned approach to improve retrieval accuracy in a domain-specific context.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper demonstrates empirically that HiPerRAG achieves state-of-the-art performance on established scientific question answering benchmarks, and also on benchmarks that it created. The performance gains over existing models and even commercial LLMs are compelling.
*   **Scalability:**  The successful scaling of HiPerRAG to large HPC systems is a significant contribution. It showcases the practical feasibility of the proposed approach for handling massive scientific datasets.
*   **Well-Defined and Executed Experiments:** The experiments are clearly outlined, and the ablation studies (e.g., on ColTrast) provide valuable insights into the effectiveness of different components.
*   **New Datasets:** The creation and release of ProteinInteractionQA, ProteinFunctionQA, and BioSynthQPs represent a valuable contribution to the community. These datasets specifically address the needs of evaluating RAG systems in the biomedical domain.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Novelty in RAG Architecture:** While the components (Oreo and ColTrast) are novel, the overall RAG architecture is fairly standard. The paper integrates these components within a well-established framework. While not necessarily a weakness, this reduces the transformative novelty of the overall system.
*   **Dependency on Specific HPC Infrastructure:**  The system's design is heavily reliant on HPC infrastructure. While this enables impressive scaling, it potentially limits its accessibility for researchers without access to such resources. The paper could benefit from a discussion of potential adaptations for more resource-constrained environments.
*   **Limited ablation of generative model influence**: The results in Table 5 only contain two models: Mistral and Mixtral. While these are strong LLMs, the extent to which these impact the overall performance of the retrieval system is not rigorously tested. A wider variety of LLMs would help to better inform future architectural decisions.

*Significance:*

The paper has the potential to be highly significant within the scientific knowledge management and AI for science domains. HiPerRAG offers a concrete and effective solution for addressing the information overload problem faced by scientists. The proposed techniques could significantly accelerate scientific discovery by improving knowledge access and synthesis. The release of new benchmarks will also spur further research in this area. The HPC-enabled scaling is also a key point; large-scale RAG applications are necessary to manage the burgeoning scientific literature.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of 8/10.

*   **A score of 9 or 10** would require more substantial architectural innovation in RAG itself or a demonstration of a truly transformative impact on scientific discovery.
*   **The score of 8** recognizes the practical significance of addressing the information overload problem in science, the well-executed implementation, the strong empirical results, the creation of new datasets, and the effective scaling of a RAG system on HPC resources. However, the limited architectural novelty in the overall RAG framework and the HPC dependency, as well as other weaknesses outlined, prevent it from reaching a higher score. While the components are novel and valuable, the overall contribution, while significant, falls short of being groundbreaking.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### CRAFT: Cultural Russian-Oriented Dataset Adaptation for Focused Text-to-Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04851v1)
- **Authors**: Viacheslav Vasilev, Vladimir Arkhipkin, Julia Agafonova, Tatiana Nikulina, Evelina Mironova, Alisa Shichanina, Nikolai Gerasimenko, Mikhail Shoytov, Denis Dimitrov
- **Abstract**: Despite the fact that popular text-to-image generation models cope well with international and general cultural queries, they have a significant knowledge gap regarding individual cultures. This is due to the content of existing large training datasets collected on the Internet, which are predominantly based on Western European or American popular culture. Meanwhile, the lack of cultural adaptation of the model can lead to incorrect results, a decrease in the generation quality, and the spread of stereotypes and offensive content. In an effort to address this issue, we examine the concept of cultural code and recognize the critical importance of its understanding by modern image generation models, an issue that has not been sufficiently addressed in the research community to date. We propose the methodology for collecting and processing the data necessary to form a dataset based on the cultural code, in particular the Russian one. We explore how the collected data affects the quality of generations in the national domain and analyze the effectiveness of our approach using the Kandinsky 3.1 text-to-image model. Human evaluation results demonstrate an increase in the level of awareness of Russian culture in the model.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CRAFT: CULTURAL RUSSIAN-ORIENTED DATASET ADAPTATION FOR FOCUSED TEXT-TO-IMAGE GENERATION":

**Summary:**
