### EcoAgent: An Efficient Edge-Cloud Collaborative Multi-Agent Framework for Mobile Automation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05440v1)
- **Authors**: Biao Yi, Xavier Hu, Yurun Chen, Shengyu Zhang, Hongxia Yang, Fan Wu, Fei Wu
- **Abstract**: Cloud-based mobile agents powered by (multimodal) large language models ((M)LLMs) offer strong reasoning abilities but suffer from high latency and cost. While fine-tuned (M)SLMs enable edge deployment, they often lose general capabilities and struggle with complex tasks. To address this, we propose EcoAgent, an Edge-Cloud cOllaborative multi-agent framework for mobile automation. EcoAgent features a closed-loop collaboration among a cloud-based Planning Agent and two edge-based agents: the Execution Agent for action execution and the Observation Agent for verifying outcomes. The Observation Agent uses a Pre-Understanding Module to compress screen images into concise text, reducing token usage. In case of failure, the Planning Agent retrieves screen history and replans via a Reflection Module. Experiments on AndroidWorld show that EcoAgent maintains high task success rates while significantly reducing MLLM token consumption, enabling efficient and practical mobile automation.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "EcoAgent: An Efficient Edge-Cloud Collaborative Multi-Agent Framework for Mobile Automation".

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EcoAgent, a framework designed to efficiently automate mobile tasks by leveraging both cloud-based and edge-based agents.  It tackles the limitations of cloud-based MLLMs (high latency, cost, lack of direct screen interaction) and edge-based MSLMs (limited reasoning, inability to handle complex tasks). EcoAgent utilizes a cloud-based Planning Agent for high-level reasoning and two edge-based agents: an Execution Agent for action execution and an Observation Agent for outcome verification. To reduce token usage, it introduces a Pre-Understanding Module to compress screen images into text. A Reflection Module helps the Planning Agent replan when failures occur, using a memory of previous screen states.  Experiments on the AndroidWorld benchmark demonstrate that EcoAgent achieves high task success rates while reducing MLLM token consumption.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of combining cloud-based reasoning with edge-based execution for mobile automation isn't entirely new; however, EcoAgent's specific architecture and modules contribute some novelty.  The combination of *three* agents each with a specific role (Planning, Execution, and Observation) is more structured than some prior approaches that focus on simpler delegation. The Pre-Understanding Module for compressing screen information is a significant contribution, as are the Memory and Reflection modules for failure recovery and adaptive planning. However, the novelty of *each* module in isolation is relatively minor; similar components may have been used in other agent systems or computer vision tasks.  The framework appears novel *as a whole*, more so than each component individually. This makes the approach very useful.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in mobile automation: balancing the need for strong reasoning (MLLMs) with the constraints of mobile devices (latency, cost, edge capabilities). Reducing the reliance on costly cloud resources by using edge-based processing is a worthwhile goal, especially for real-world deployment. Demonstrating a reduction in token consumption is also significant for practical usability and cost-effectiveness. The experiments on AndroidWorld offer a realistic evaluation environment, increasing the paper's significance. The ablation studies, if thorough, further strengthen the paper by validating the contribution of each component. The improvement upon the baselines, especially considering the significantly lowered cost, demonstrates potential.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly outlines the limitations of existing cloud-based and edge-based approaches to mobile automation.
    * **Well-Defined Framework:** EcoAgent's architecture is clearly explained, and the roles of each agent and module are well-defined.
    * **Practical Focus:** The focus on reducing token consumption and latency highlights the practical applicability of the framework.
    * **Experimental Validation:** Using AndroidWorld provides a realistic and standardized evaluation environment.
    * **Ablation Studies:** The inclusion of ablation studies is a strong point, providing insights into the importance of each component.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of components is novel, the individual components themselves are not groundbreaking.
    * **Dependency on specific MLLMs:** The results rely on specific GPT-4o APIs and MSLMs available as of the paper's submission. The rapidly evolving landscape of language models may affect the reproducibility or generalizability of the results.
    * **Limited Generalizability of Edge Agents**: Using a Qwen2-VL for object recognition may affect generalizability. The MSLMs used in execution may not be available to all, which is an issue.
    * **Lack of Qualitative Analysis:** While quantitative results are provided, the paper lacks a qualitative analysis of the types of errors that EcoAgent still makes and how the Reflection Module handles specific failure scenarios.  This would add more depth to the evaluation. A visualization or more in-depth example would add clarity.

**Justification for Score:**

EcoAgent presents a well-engineered and practically motivated solution to a relevant problem in mobile automation. The combination of cloud and edge agents, along with the added modules for pre-understanding and reflection, create a more robust and efficient system than previous approaches. The experimental results are promising.

However, the novelty is somewhat incremental. The individual modules, while useful, are not revolutionary on their own. There is also a dependency on specific, current, language model APIs. The reliance on proprietary model APIs also increases the risk of irreproducibility or performance degradation if those APIs change. The lack of comprehensive qualitative analysis also impacts the rating.

Therefore, considering the positive aspects but also the limitations of novelty and scope, a score of 7 is appropriate.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### clem:todd: A Framework for the Systematic Benchmarking of LLM-Based Task-Oriented Dialogue System Realisations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05445v1)
- **Authors**: Chalamalasetti Kranti, Sherzod Hakimov, David Schlangen
- **Abstract**: The emergence of instruction-tuned large language models (LLMs) has advanced the field of dialogue systems, enabling both realistic user simulations and robust multi-turn conversational agents. However, existing research often evaluates these components in isolation-either focusing on a single user simulator or a specific system design-limiting the generalisability of insights across architectures and configurations. In this work, we propose clem todd (chat-optimized LLMs for task-oriented dialogue systems development), a flexible framework for systematically evaluating dialogue systems under consistent conditions. clem todd enables detailed benchmarking across combinations of user simulators and dialogue systems, whether existing models from literature or newly developed ones. It supports plug-and-play integration and ensures uniform datasets, evaluation metrics, and computational constraints. We showcase clem todd's flexibility by re-evaluating existing task-oriented dialogue systems within this unified setup and integrating three newly proposed dialogue systems into the same evaluation pipeline. Our results provide actionable insights into how architecture, scale, and prompting strategies affect dialogue performance, offering practical guidance for building efficient and effective conversational AI systems.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "clem: todd: A Framework for the Systematic Benchmarking of LLM-Based Task-Oriented Dialogue System Realisations":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces clem: todd, a novel framework for systematically benchmarking LLM-based Task-Oriented Dialogue (TOD) systems. It adopts a self-play setup, pitting user simulators (Player A) against dialogue systems (Player B), orchestrated by a game master. This framework enables consistent evaluation across various user simulator and dialogue system combinations, facilitating detailed analysis of architectural choices, model sizes, and prompting strategies. The authors showcase clem: todd by re-evaluating existing TOD systems and integrating newly developed ones, providing insights into dialogue performance, task success, and computational cost. They address the limitations of existing TOD evaluation methods by offering a unified setup that mitigates inconsistent metrics and compute settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a crucial gap in the field by providing a unified framework for evaluating LLM-based TOD systems. Existing evaluations are often fragmented, making cross-system comparisons difficult. Clem: todd offers a more standardized and systematic approach. The use of self-play for evaluating TOD systems is a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** Clem: todd enables more reliable and reproducible research in the field of TOD systems, by facilitating detailed benchmarking and providing insights into architectural choices and system interactions. This is particularly significant given the rapid evolution of LLMs and the increasing complexity of dialogue system architectures. The framework also allows exploration into areas of system robustness and generalizability which are often glossed over in individual system design papers. The tool has the potential to standardize evaluation in the field.

*   **Experimental Rigor:** The authors conduct extensive experiments, re-evaluating existing systems and integrating new ones, within their framework. The inclusion of both open-weight and closed-weight LLMs allows for a broad analysis of performance tradeoffs. The clear articulation of the experimental setup and evaluation metrics enhances the reproducibility of the results. The framework is built with integration of new systems and user simulators in mind, suggesting a reusable and adaptable tool for researchers to explore.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Scope of Evaluation:** While clem: todd provides a valuable framework, the evaluation is largely confined to the MultiWOZ 2.2 dataset with a restriction to booking tasks. While filtering out irrelevant tasks creates a focused evaluation, it also impacts the breadth of use cases assessed in the paper. The new synthetic datasets expand the domain but also introduce the caveat of unrealistic and artificial settings. A broader evaluation across various datasets and task types would further strengthen the framework's generalizability.

*   **Complexity of Systems:** The framework includes both existing and new dialogue system configurations. The modularity of the framework may mean the underlying complexity to the systems or components being integrated, are not fully captured or addressed. This may lead to a situation where a simpler, monolithic system achieves a higher score than it may deserve.

*   **Reliance on LLMs for Evaluation:** The evaluation relies on LLMs (e.g., GPT-4o) for assessing aspects like dialogue quality and naturalness. While LLMs as judges are increasingly common, they are not without biases and limitations. Human evaluation, beyond the Turing test, for aspects like task success could enhance the reliability of the evaluation. The framework also assumes all goals can be assessed without human judgement.

*   **Limitations in the Self-Play Setup:** The reliance on adherence to the Tool Schema, while enforcing format compliance, can lead to premature termination of dialogues and underestimate the capabilities of certain models. The two-player interactions, while a starting point, may not fully capture the complexities of real-world dialogue scenarios. There also seems to be limited user customisability and control in current framework, limiting potential insights into agent behaviours to specific prompt and parameters.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by introducing a much-needed framework for systematically benchmarking LLM-based TOD systems. While the current evaluation is somewhat limited in scope and there are inherent limitations to the self-play set-up, the clem: todd framework establishes a foundation for more reliable and reproducible research in the field. It also fosters research on system interactions and robustness. Considering the novelty, potential impact, and experimental rigor, but also taking into account the limitations in scope and dependence on LLMs for evaluation, a score of 8 is appropriate.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Conversational Process Model Redesign
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05453v1)
- **Authors**: Nataliia Klievtsova, Timotheus Kampik, Juergen Mangler, Stefanie Rinderle-Ma
- **Abstract**: With the recent success of large language models (LLMs), the idea of AI-augmented Business Process Management systems is becoming more feasible. One of their essential characteristics is the ability to be conversationally actionable, allowing humans to interact with the LLM effectively to perform crucial process life cycle tasks such as process model design and redesign. However, most current research focuses on single-prompt execution and evaluation of results, rather than on continuous interaction between the user and the LLM. In this work, we aim to explore the feasibility of using LLMs to empower domain experts in the creation and redesign of process models in an iterative and effective way. The proposed conversational process model redesign (CPD) approach receives as input a process model and a redesign request by the user in natural language. Instead of just letting the LLM make changes, the LLM is employed to (a) identify process change patterns from literature, (b) re-phrase the change request to be aligned with an expected wording for the identified pattern (i.e., the meaning), and then to (c) apply the meaning of the change to the process model. This multi-step approach allows for explainable and reproducible changes. In order to ensure the feasibility of the CPD approach, and to find out how well the patterns from literature can be handled by the LLM, we performed an extensive evaluation. The results show that some patterns are hard to understand by LLMs and by users. Within the scope of the study, we demonstrated that users need support to describe the changes clearly. Overall the evaluation shows that the LLMs can handle most changes well according to a set of completeness and correctness criteria.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the feasibility of using Large Language Models (LLMs) to assist domain experts in process model redesign through conversational interaction.  It introduces a Conversational Process Model Redesign (CPD) approach that receives a process model and a redesign request as input. The LLM is used to identify process change patterns from the literature, re-phrase the redesign request to align with these patterns, and then apply the changes to the process model.  The paper emphasizes explainable and reproducible changes through this multi-step process.  It evaluates the CPD approach using a user study and assesses the quality of redesigned models based on completeness, correctness, layouting, and graphical representation. The study results highlight which change patterns are difficult for LLMs (and users) to understand, and demonstrates the need for clearer user support in describing changes. The evaluation concludes that LLMs can handle most changes well according to the defined criteria. The work expands the set of change patterns and adapts them to a conversational context.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic approach to applying LLMs for conversational *redesign* of process models, rather than single-prompt creation. While prior work has investigated LLMs in process modeling, this research focuses specifically on the iterative, user-guided *redesign* aspect and incorporating established change patterns from the process modeling literature. Introducing an LLM-guided tool for adapting existing process models based on established change patterns provides a nuanced contribution. While the component technologies (LLMs, change patterns) are not individually novel, the specific combination and application to conversational process redesign has not been thoroughly explored before. The proposal of new change patterns based on observations in the conversational setting further adds to the incremental novelty.

**Significance:** The potential impact of this research is considerable. By empowering domain experts to redesign process models conversationally, it reduces the communication gap between business users and process modelers.  This could lead to more efficient process improvement cycles and better alignment of process models with real-world business needs. The study provides practical insights into how well LLMs can handle different types of process changes and what kind of user support is needed.  However, the significance is limited by the scale and complexity of the process models used in the evaluation.  While the paper acknowledges this limitation, it needs to address scalability issues better to demonstrate its real-world applicability. Furthermore, the focus on BPMN limits the applicability to other modeling paradigms.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly identifies the communication gap between domain experts and process modelers in process redesign.
*   **Well-defined approach:** The CPD approach is well-structured and clearly explained.
*   **Systematic evaluation:** The paper provides a systematic evaluation using a user study and well-defined evaluation criteria.
*   **Practical insights:** The study provides practical insights into the capabilities and limitations of LLMs in process model redesign.
*   **Extends existing patterns:** Introduces new and adapts existing change patterns to the context of LLM-driven conversational process redesign.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited model complexity:** The evaluation uses relatively simple process models, which limits the generalizability of the results to more complex real-world scenarios.  The paper needs to demonstrate how the approach scales to more intricate processes.
*   **Lack of interactivity:** The user study lacks real-time interaction with an LLM agent, potentially affecting user behavior and the accuracy of the results. The simulation is too far from a genuine interaction with the LLM.
*   **BPMN focus:** The work is limited to BPMN, while other process modeling languages are not supported.
*   **Zero-shot approach:** While justifiable, the LLMs could likely perform better with a few-shot approach to the prompting. The paper could explore approaches to few-shot learning or even fine-tuning.
*   **Limited details on prompt engineering:** While the paper alludes to system instructions, more explicit description around how the prompts were crafted would be beneficial.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the incremental novelty and the limitations in scope and evaluation (simple models, simulated interaction, etc.) the paper warrants a score that reflects its potential while acknowledging its current shortcomings. The focus on *redesign*, the integration of change patterns, and the systematic evaluation are positive aspects. However, the limitations in demonstrating real-world applicability and the lack of genuine user interaction with an LLM, prevent a higher score.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### UKElectionNarratives: A Dataset of Misleading Narratives Surrounding Recent UK General Elections
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05459v1)
- **Authors**: Fatima Haouari, Carolina Scarton, Nicolò Faggiani, Nikolaos Nikolaidis, Bonka Kotseva, Ibrahim Abu Farha, Jens Linge, Kalina Bontcheva
- **Abstract**: Misleading narratives play a crucial role in shaping public opinion during elections, as they can influence how voters perceive candidates and political parties. This entails the need to detect these narratives accurately. To address this, we introduce the first taxonomy of common misleading narratives that circulated during recent elections in Europe. Based on this taxonomy, we construct and analyse UKElectionNarratives: the first dataset of human-annotated misleading narratives which circulated during the UK General Elections in 2019 and 2024. We also benchmark Pre-trained and Large Language Models (focusing on GPT-4o), studying their effectiveness in detecting election-related misleading narratives. Finally, we discuss potential use cases and make recommendations for future research directions using the proposed codebook and dataset.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "UKElectionNarratives: A Dataset of Misleading Narratives Surrounding Recent UK General Elections".

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel dataset called UKElectionNarratives, which consists of human-annotated social media posts (tweets) containing misleading narratives related to the 2019 and 2024 UK General Elections. The authors first develop a multi-level taxonomy of common misleading narratives prevalent during European elections. This taxonomy then serves as the basis for annotating the dataset. The authors also benchmark the performance of pre-trained language models (PLMs) and large language models (LLMs), specifically GPT-4o, on the task of detecting these misleading narratives. The paper concludes by discussing potential use cases of the dataset and offering suggestions for future research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents some important and relevant novelty. The construction of a labeled dataset of misleading narratives surrounding UK elections is a valuable contribution. While other datasets exist related to misinformation, the specific focus on election narratives and a detailed, multi-level taxonomy is a new aspect. The benchmark testing of PLMs and LLMs on this dataset adds another layer of novelty, providing insights into their ability to address this specific challenge.

**Significance:** The significance of the work stems from the increasing importance of addressing misinformation and misleading narratives, especially during elections. The dataset can aid in developing more accurate and effective methods for detecting and countering harmful narratives that influence public opinion and potentially undermine democratic processes. The analysis of LLMs provides a practical direction in automating this detection. This study also highlights the types of narratives found in the UK context, providing a point of comparison for research in other countries.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined taxonomy:** The paper presents a literature-backed, multi-level taxonomy that captures common misleading narratives in European elections.
*   **Human-annotated dataset:** The dataset is created through human annotation, which is more reliable than automated methods. They also had a three-stage process to solve disagreements in annotation.
*   **Benchmark experiments:** The paper provides benchmark results for several PLMs and LLMs, offering valuable insights for future research.
*   **Potential Use Cases:** The authors discuss a range of potential use cases for the dataset, which is useful for the research community.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited scope:** While the UK-focus allows depth, it limits the generalizability of the findings to other contexts. The narrative may differ in different elections.
*   **Dataset size:** While reasonable, a larger dataset would further improve the reliability of the benchmark experiments and training of machine learning models. The dataset is also unbalanced, with some narrative classes having very few examples.
*   **Agreement score in Stage 1:** The agreement score is fair agreement but is still very low for the data to be accurate for training on it.
*   **Lack of error analysis:** A detailed error analysis of the machine learning models would be valuable to understand the types of errors they make and suggest directions for improvement.
*   **Lack of generalization of LLMs:** There are better few-shot techniques for training LLMs which would likely produce higher numbers.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a good contribution in a very relevant area of research. The dataset provides a concrete resource to spur more research in narrative detection for elections. Its importance is enhanced by a well-defined methodology and benchmarking of contemporary LLMs. However, the paper is brought down due to a number of weaknesses. Its limited scope and dataset size restricts the impact. Also, some of their decisions for creating the data could be improved.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Bring Reason to Vision: Understanding Perception and Reasoning through Model Merging
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05464v1)
- **Authors**: Shiqi Chen, Jinghan Zhang, Tongyao Zhu, Wei Liu, Siyang Gao, Miao Xiong, Manling Li, Junxian He
- **Abstract**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) combine visual perception with the general capabilities, such as reasoning, of Large Language Models (LLMs). However, the mechanisms by which these two abilities can be combined and contribute remain poorly understood. In this work, we explore to compose perception and reasoning through model merging that connects parameters of different models. Unlike previous works that often focus on merging models of the same kind, we propose merging models across modalities, enabling the incorporation of the reasoning capabilities of LLMs into VLMs. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate that model merging offers a successful pathway to transfer reasoning abilities from LLMs to VLMs in a training-free manner. Moreover, we utilize the merged models to understand the internal mechanism of perception and reasoning and how merging affects it. We find that perception capabilities are predominantly encoded in the early layers of the model, whereas reasoning is largely facilitated by the middle-to-late layers. After merging, we observe that all layers begin to contribute to reasoning, whereas the distribution of perception abilities across layers remains largely unchanged. These observations shed light on the potential of model merging as a tool for multimodal integration and interpretation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Bring Reason to Vision: Understanding Perception and Reasoning through Model Merging":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates how to enhance the reasoning capabilities of Vision-Language Models (VLMs) by merging them with Large Language Models (LLMs) that are specifically trained for math reasoning. Unlike previous model merging approaches that focus on models of the same modality, this work explores merging across modalities. The core idea is to transfer the reasoning abilities of LLMs into VLMs without requiring additional training, by performing a weighted average of the parameters of the language model component of the VLM and the reasoning-specialized LLM. Through experiments on multiple math-related benchmarks, the authors demonstrate that this model merging strategy consistently improves performance. The paper also delves into understanding how perception and reasoning are embedded within the merged models, using knockout analysis to show that perception abilities reside predominantly in early layers, while reasoning is facilitated by later layers.  After merging, reasoning abilities are seen to influence most layers in the model, while the layer-wise distribution of perception ability remains relatively stable.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in applying model merging techniques *across different modalities* (LLMs and VLMs) to enhance reasoning abilities. While model merging itself is not new, its application to improve VLMs by incorporating LLM-specific reasoning capabilities and, more importantly, analyzing the resulting parameter space, is a valuable contribution. The in-depth interpretability analysis, pinpointing where perception and reasoning are located in the layers of the model is also a notable element of novelty. The approach isn't entirely revolutionary, but it's a logical extension of existing work with a strong empirical focus and useful insights.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation of VLMs, namely their relative weakness in reasoning compared to pure language models. By successfully transferring reasoning abilities through model merging, it offers a potentially significant and training-free pathway for improving VLM performance. The interpretability analysis also adds to the broader understanding of how multimodal models encode and combine different capabilities. This improved understanding of parameter spaces could lead to more efficient training strategies in the future. The consistent improvement shown across different models and datasets reinforces its robustness. However, the impact is somewhat limited by focusing primarily on math reasoning.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the reasoning bottleneck in VLMs and motivates the need for improvement.
    *   **Effective Approach:** Model merging is a simple yet effective technique for achieving the desired transfer of reasoning abilities.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The paper includes a wide range of experiments across different models, datasets, and tasks, lending credence to its findings.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The knockout analysis provides valuable insights into the internal mechanisms of VLMs and how model merging affects the interplay of perception and reasoning.
    *   **Open Source:** The provided link to the code enhances reproducibility and facilitates further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The focus on math reasoning, while useful for demonstrating the concept, limits the generalizability of the findings. It remains unclear whether the same techniques and insights would apply to other types of reasoning (e.g., common sense, logical).
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While consistent, the improvements observed are often incremental rather than transformative. Some benefits seen are also specific to certain architectures or task categories.
    *   **Methodological Simplification:** The choice of *linear merging*, while justified by simplicity, could potentially limit the performance gains achievable. More advanced merging techniques might yield even better results.
    *   **Lack of Deeper Theoretical Foundation:** The paper provides an empirical understanding but lacks a deeper theoretical framework for explaining why model merging works in this cross-modal setting.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper opens up avenues for future research in several directions:
    *   Exploring model merging for transferring other types of skills across modalities.
    *   Developing more sophisticated merging techniques tailored to the specific characteristics of VLMs and LLMs.
    *   Investigating the potential of model merging for improving the robustness and generalization abilities of VLMs.
    *   Using the interpretability techniques developed in this paper to better understand the internal workings of other multimodal models.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a valuable and well-executed study, its novelty and significance are somewhat constrained by its limited scope and incremental improvements. The central idea of cross-modal merging is well-executed, and the findings related to the layer-wise distribution of abilities is insightful. However, there's still substantial room for future work to broaden the scope and deepen the theoretical understanding. Given the limitations in the scope of the experiments (focused on math), the incremental nature of some of the improvements, and the simplicity of the merging technique, a score of 6 reflects the paper's strengths as a solid, insightful contribution to the field while acknowledging the areas where further investigation is warranted.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### ComPO: Preference Alignment via Comparison Oracles
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05465v1)
- **Authors**: Peter Chen, Xi Chen, Wotao Yin, Tianyi Lin
- **Abstract**: Direct alignment methods are increasingly used for aligning large language models (LLMs) with human preferences. However, these methods suffer from the issues of verbosity and likelihood displacement, which can be driven by the noisy preference pairs that induce similar likelihood for preferred and dispreferred responses. The contributions of this paper are two-fold. First, we propose a new preference alignment method based on comparison oracles and provide the convergence guarantee for its basic scheme. Second, we improve our method using some heuristics and conduct the experiments to demonstrate the flexibility and compatibility of practical scheme in improving the performance of LLMs using noisy preference pairs. Evaluations are conducted across multiple base and instruction-tuned models (Mistral-7B, Llama-3-8B and Gemma-2-9B) with benchmarks (AlpacaEval 2, MT-Bench and Arena-Hard). Experimental results show the effectiveness of our method as an alternative to addressing the limitations of existing direct alignment methods. A highlight of our work is that we evidence the importance of designing specialized methods for preference pairs with distinct likelihood margin, which complements the recent findings in \citet{Razin-2025-Unintentional}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "ComPO: Preference Alignment via Comparison Oracles" introduces a new direct alignment method for large language models (LLMs) based on comparison oracles. This method addresses the verbosity and likelihood displacement issues often encountered in direct alignment methods like DPO. The core idea is to leverage noisy preference pairs (pairs that induce similar likelihoods for preferred and dispreferred responses) by treating them as oracle outputs guiding model parameter updates. The paper presents a basic scheme with convergence guarantees and then enhances it with practical heuristics for large-scale model fine-tuning, including integration with DPO and efficient approximations of gradient estimation. Experiments across various base and instruction-tuned models and benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application of comparison oracles to direct preference alignment in LLMs. While comparison oracles have been used in other optimization contexts, their adaptation to the specific challenges of preference alignment, particularly in handling noisy data, is a significant contribution. The idea of treating preference pairs as inherently providing comparative judgments based on a latent alignment goal, rather than forcing an explicit optimization against a potentially flawed proxy objective function (like DPO's log-likelihood margin), represents a genuinely new perspective. The theoretical justification, though based on adapting existing results, further strengthens the novelty. It is, however, an evolutionary rather than revolutionary advancement compared to DPO.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical improvements demonstrated in the experiments. Alleviating verbosity and likelihood displacement are crucial for deploying robust and trustworthy LLMs. The ability to leverage noisy preference data, which are often discarded, offers increased data efficiency. The experimental results showcasing improvements in length-controlled win rate (LC) and overall win rate (WR) across several benchmarks are compelling. The comparative results in terms of average performance are welcome, which provides a sense of robustness. Also, the findings support the importance of specialized methods for preference pairs with distinct likelihood margins, as evidenced by the findings from Razin et al. [71]. The combination of ComPO with DPO and SimPO highlights its flexibility and compatibility with existing direct alignment methods.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong theoretical foundation providing convergence guarantees (though under somewhat restrictive conditions).
    *   Effective handling of noisy preference pairs.
    *   Practical enhancements for large-scale model fine-tuning.
    *   Demonstrated improvements in verbosity and likelihood displacement.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple models and benchmarks.
    *   Clear and well-written paper.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The basic scheme has limited practicality due to high dimensionality. While the authors acknowledge this and provide a practical approximation, more insights could be provided into the sensitivity of the method to the tuning parameters of the practical scheme.
    *   The margin threshold parameter for splitting data into clean and noisy pairs requires careful tuning. An adaptive method for selecting this threshold could improve robustness.
    *   The theoretical analysis makes strong assumptions about the existence and smoothness of the "true" alignment objective function, which is hard to verify in practice.
    *   While providing a better handling of noisy preferences, the improvements obtained are incremental, and in some cases, other methods can still outperform the approach (e.g., Arena-Hard for Mistral-Instruct-7B), which somewhat diminishes the overall robustness argument.
    * Lack of direct comparison with the method of Razin et al. that filters out preference pairs with small CHES score.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in direct preference alignment by highlighting the importance of comparison oracles and robust handling of noisy data. It encourages researchers to explore alternative alignment objectives beyond simple log-likelihood maximization and to develop more data-efficient and robust training methods. The released models can foster further research in this direction. The work is particularly interesting because it offers a possible alternative to RLHF.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel adaptation of comparison oracles for direct preference alignment, addressing a critical limitation of existing methods related to noisy data. The theoretical justification and practical enhancements contribute to a solid and well-executed research effort. While the improvements are not revolutionary and limitations still remain, the paper provides valuable insights and a potentially significant alternative approach to LLM alignment with good empirical support and some theoretical backing that warrants a score above average. Also, the flexibility of the approach is well-demonstrated with SimPO and DPO integration. All in all, the paper is very valuable for the community.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mogao: An Omni Foundation Model for Interleaved Multi-Modal Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05472v1)
- **Authors**: Chao Liao, Liyang Liu, Xun Wang, Zhengxiong Luo, Xinyu Zhang, Wenliang Zhao, Jie Wu, Liang Li, Zhi Tian, Weilin Huang
- **Abstract**: Recent progress in unified models for image understanding and generation has been impressive, yet most approaches remain limited to single-modal generation conditioned on multiple modalities. In this paper, we present Mogao, a unified framework that advances this paradigm by enabling interleaved multi-modal generation through a causal approach. Mogao integrates a set of key technical improvements in architecture design, including a deep-fusion design, dual vision encoders, interleaved rotary position embeddings, and multi-modal classifier-free guidance, which allow it to harness the strengths of both autoregressive models for text generation and diffusion models for high-quality image synthesis. These practical improvements also make Mogao particularly effective to process interleaved sequences of text and images arbitrarily. To further unlock the potential of unified models, we introduce an efficient training strategy on a large-scale, in-house dataset specifically curated for joint text and image generation. Extensive experiments show that Mogao not only achieves state-of-the-art performance in multi-modal understanding and text-to-image generation, but also excels in producing high-quality, coherent interleaved outputs. Its emergent capabilities in zero-shot image editing and compositional generation highlight Mogao as a practical omni-modal foundation model, paving the way for future development and scaling the unified multi-modal systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Mogao, a unified framework for interleaved multi-modal generation. Unlike existing multi-modal models that are typically limited to single-modal generation conditioned on multiple modalities, Mogao enables the interleaved generation of text and images. The framework uses a causal approach and integrates several technical improvements, including a deep-fusion design, dual vision encoders (VAE and ViT), interleaved rotary position embeddings, and multi-modal classifier-free guidance. The model is trained on a large-scale, in-house dataset designed for joint text and image generation. The results indicate that Mogao achieves state-of-the-art performance in multi-modal understanding and text-to-image generation and shows emergent capabilities in zero-shot image editing and compositional generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the architecture that efficiently integrates auto-regressive text generation with diffusion-based image generation in an *interleaved* manner.  The combined use of the deep-fusion design, dual vision encoders, and interleaved RoPE contributes to the performance.  The training strategy that handles interleaved sequences and the dual CFG mechanism address specific challenges in this interleaved context. The combination of these elements, specifically geared towards interleaved output, is arguably novel.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to advance multi-modal interaction and communication. The ability to generate text and images in a flowing, interleaved way is a key step towards more natural and human-like AI systems.  The zero-shot editing results are also promising, indicating the model's ability to adapt to new tasks. The work represents a practical step towards more complex multi-modal foundation models. This capability addresses a key gap in current generative models which often restrict outputs to one modality despite multi-modal conditioning.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The interleaved multi-modal generation capability is a valuable contribution.
    *   The detailed architectural design choices and training strategies are well-motivated.
    *   The extensive experiments demonstrate strong performance across different tasks.
    *   The ablation studies justify the design decisions made for Mogao.
    *   Competitive performance is demonstrated on standard benchmarks, including human evaluations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper mentions using a large-scale, in-house dataset, but provides little detail. This lack of transparency makes it difficult to reproduce the results and assess the model's potential biases or limitations. The dataset composition is a major factor in the quality of generated content.
    *   While the architecture is well-engineered, the individual components (AR, Diffusion, ViT, VAE, RoPE) are not fundamentally new. It is more of an integration and optimization of existing components which, while valuable, lowers the novelty score.
    *   The reliance on Qwen2.5 as a backbone means the method is tied to that specific architecture and implementation.
    *   The focus is predominantly on quantitative results. The paper could benefit from deeper qualitative analysis, showcasing failure cases and limitations.  A more critical examination of potential biases in the generated content would also be beneficial.

**Overall:**

Mogao represents a solid engineering effort to create a practical and effective interleaved multi-modal generation framework. The work builds on existing components, but combines them in a novel architecture and training regime targeted at the specific challenge of generating interleaved text and images. The performance is strong and the ablation studies provide valuable insights. However, the lack of transparency regarding the in-house dataset and the reliance on existing techniques limit the potential for groundbreaking impact. While the interleaved multimodal capabilities are a significant step forward, the incremental nature of innovation warrants a lower score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DiffusionSfM: Predicting Structure and Motion via Ray Origin and Endpoint Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05473v1)
- **Authors**: Qitao Zhao, Amy Lin, Jeff Tan, Jason Y. Zhang, Deva Ramanan, Shubham Tulsiani
- **Abstract**: Current Structure-from-Motion (SfM) methods typically follow a two-stage pipeline, combining learned or geometric pairwise reasoning with a subsequent global optimization step. In contrast, we propose a data-driven multi-view reasoning approach that directly infers 3D scene geometry and camera poses from multi-view images. Our framework, DiffusionSfM, parameterizes scene geometry and cameras as pixel-wise ray origins and endpoints in a global frame and employs a transformer-based denoising diffusion model to predict them from multi-view inputs. To address practical challenges in training diffusion models with missing data and unbounded scene coordinates, we introduce specialized mechanisms that ensure robust learning. We empirically validate DiffusionSfM on both synthetic and real datasets, demonstrating that it outperforms classical and learning-based approaches while naturally modeling uncertainty.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "DiffusionSfM: Predicting Structure and Motion via Ray Origin and Endpoint Diffusion" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiffusionSfM, a novel end-to-end multi-view structure-from-motion (SfM) approach that predicts 3D scene geometry and camera poses directly from multi-view images using a denoising diffusion model.  Instead of the traditional SfM pipeline that separates pairwise reasoning and global optimization, DiffusionSfM unifies these into a single framework by parameterizing scene geometry and cameras as pixel-wise ray origins and endpoints in a global coordinate frame.  The method addresses the challenges of training diffusion models with missing depth data and unbounded scene coordinates by introducing specialized techniques, including GT mask conditioning and parameterizing 3D points in projective space. Experiments on synthetic and real datasets demonstrate that DiffusionSfM outperforms classical and learning-based SfM approaches, while naturally modeling uncertainty.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant departure from the standard two-stage SfM pipeline. The key novelty lies in:

    *   **End-to-end learning via diffusion:** Directly predicting scene geometry and camera poses using a diffusion model without relying on pairwise matching and global optimization. This unification is a substantial shift.
    *   **Ray origin and endpoint parameterization:**  Representing scene geometry and cameras through pixel-wise ray origins and endpoints is a clever way to couple geometry and pose estimation, enabling joint reasoning. It also provides a more comprehensive representation than just camera poses (as in RayDiffusion [42]) or point clouds (as in DUSt3R [37]).
    *   **Training strategies for diffusion in SfM:** The paper introduces specific techniques to handle incomplete GT data (GT mask conditioning) and unbounded scene coordinates (homogeneous parameterization) for training diffusion models, making the method practical.
    *   **Sparse-to-dense training:** The proposed sparse-to-dense training approach helps to improve convergence and performance by pre-training a sparse model and using it to initialize a dense model. This tackles the computational challenges of training a high-resolution diffusion model from scratch.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of DiffusionSfM on the field is considerable because:

    *   **Improved Accuracy:** The method demonstrates improved camera pose estimation compared to existing learning-based and classical approaches on standard datasets, suggesting a leap forward in SfM performance. In particular, the camera center accuracy improvement is noteworthy and appears directly linked to the explicit ray origin modeling.
    *   **Uncertainty Modeling:** Leveraging a diffusion model allows for inherent uncertainty modeling, which is important for applications requiring robust SfM in real-world scenarios. The ability to generate multiple plausible scene interpretations (as illustrated by the vase example) is valuable.
    *   **Scalability and Adaptability:** The end-to-end nature simplifies the SfM pipeline and offers potential for greater scalability and adaptation to different sensor modalities (e.g., incorporating LiDAR).
    *   **Alternative Representation:** Provides the community with a novel alternative representation for cameras and geometry, potentially spurring new research directions for multi-view geometry.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Thorough experiments on various datasets.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition and Solutions:** The paper identifies and addresses key challenges related to training diffusion models for SfM.
    *   **Well-Written:** Easy to follow and understand the method's details.
    *   **Ablation Studies:**  Demonstrate the importance of different components of the method (GT mask, homogeneous coordinates).
    *   **Strong Performance:** Outperforms many existing methods for camera center estimation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models are inherently computationally expensive. The iterative denoising process, while offering uncertainty modeling benefits, significantly increases inference time compared to direct regression methods. However, the paper addresses this to some extent via early stopping.
    *   **Pixel-Space Diffusion Limitation:** The paper notes the potential limitation of using a pixel-space diffusion model. Moving to a latent space could improve efficiency and reduce noise in outputs, but is left as future work.
    *   **Still reliant on strong features:** Model uses DINOv2, this could mask other limitations in the approach as DINOv2 is extremely powerful on its own.
    *   **Reliance on pre-computed pairs**: The approach still requires pre-computed pairs of images to iteratively construct the multi-view dataset.
    *   **Limited ablation**: The performance of the sparse model is not clearly discussed. Further analysis would be very insightful.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to significantly influence the field of SfM. The end-to-end diffusion approach, combined with the ray origin/endpoint parameterization, provides a strong foundation for future research on:

    *   **Integrating other sensor data:** Combining images with LiDAR or other depth sensors within the diffusion framework.
    *   **Learning expressive latent spaces:**  Developing more efficient latent space diffusion models for SfM.
    *   **Adaptive sampling strategies:**  Improving the scalability of the method by using more efficient sampling techniques for the diffusion process.
    *   **Application in other geometric vision tasks:** Extending the framework to tackle related geometric vision tasks, such as visual SLAM or 3D scene reconstruction.

*   **Score Justification:**

    The paper introduces a novel and significant approach to SfM that has the potential to reshape the field. The end-to-end diffusion approach is a notable departure from traditional methods. The strengths mentioned above are compelling. The weaknesses, while present, are adequately acknowledged and provide directions for future research. The improvement in performance metrics is substantial, and the uncertainty modeling is an added benefit. For these reasons, I assign a score of 8. This reflects the strong novelty, the potential impact, and the solid empirical validation, balanced by the computational cost and some limitations that present opportunities for future work.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### 3D Scene Generation: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05474v1)
- **Authors**: Beichen Wen, Haozhe Xie, Zhaoxi Chen, Fangzhou Hong, Ziwei Liu
- **Abstract**: 3D scene generation seeks to synthesize spatially structured, semantically meaningful, and photorealistic environments for applications such as immersive media, robotics, autonomous driving, and embodied AI. Early methods based on procedural rules offered scalability but limited diversity. Recent advances in deep generative models (e.g., GANs, diffusion models) and 3D representations (e.g., NeRF, 3D Gaussians) have enabled the learning of real-world scene distributions, improving fidelity, diversity, and view consistency. Recent advances like diffusion models bridge 3D scene synthesis and photorealism by reframing generation as image or video synthesis problems. This survey provides a systematic overview of state-of-the-art approaches, organizing them into four paradigms: procedural generation, neural 3D-based generation, image-based generation, and video-based generation. We analyze their technical foundations, trade-offs, and representative results, and review commonly used datasets, evaluation protocols, and downstream applications. We conclude by discussing key challenges in generation capacity, 3D representation, data and annotations, and evaluation, and outline promising directions including higher fidelity, physics-aware and interactive generation, and unified perception-generation models. This review organizes recent advances in 3D scene generation and highlights promising directions at the intersection of generative AI, 3D vision, and embodied intelligence. To track ongoing developments, we maintain an up-to-date project page: https://github.com/hzxie/Awesome-3D-Scene-Generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive survey of 3D scene generation techniques. It categorizes existing methods into four paradigms: procedural generation, neural 3D-based generation, image-based generation, and video-based generation. The survey analyzes the technical foundations, trade-offs, and representative results of each approach. It also reviews commonly used datasets, evaluation protocols, and downstream applications. The paper concludes by discussing key challenges in generation capacity, 3D representation, data and annotations, and evaluation. It also outlines promising future directions, including higher fidelity, physics-aware and interactive generation, and unified perception-generation models.  The paper maintains an up-to-date project page to track ongoing developments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its comprehensive coverage and systematic organization of the rapidly evolving field of 3D scene generation. While individual components (e.g., GANs, NeRFs, diffusion models) are not novel, the holistic view and structured categorization across the four paradigms contribute meaningfully to the field. It addresses a gap in existing literature by providing a consolidated overview, whereas prior surveys focused on narrow subdomains or specific aspects of 3D or 4D generation. The inclusion of video-based generation and dynamic scene representations is a valuable addition that reflects recent trends.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant for several reasons. First, it provides a valuable resource for researchers entering the field of 3D scene generation. It serves as a roadmap, guiding readers through the diverse approaches, datasets, and evaluation metrics. Second, it highlights the key challenges and future directions, which can stimulate future research and innovation. The identification of areas such as physics-aware generation and unified perception-generation models is particularly valuable. Third, it provides a structured taxonomy that is helpful for understanding the landscape and comparing different methods. The inclusion of downstream applications demonstrates the practical relevance of this area of research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a wide range of techniques, including both traditional and recent methods.
    *   **Systematic Organization:** The classification into four paradigms is clear and well-defined.
    *   **Balanced Analysis:** The paper provides a fair and balanced assessment of the strengths and weaknesses of each approach.
    *   **Future Directions:** The discussion of challenges and future directions is insightful and thought-provoking.
    *   **Up-to-date:** The presence of a continually updated project page enhances the long-term value.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Depth of Analysis:** While broad, the survey can sometimes lack in-depth analysis of the mathematical or algorithmic details of specific methods.
    *   **Subjectivity:** The categorization might be open to interpretation in some cases. While the chosen categorization provides a clear structure, some papers might fit into multiple categories.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** The discussion of evaluation metrics could be more critical, highlighting the limitations of current metrics and advocating for more robust evaluation strategies.

*   **Potential Influence:** This survey is likely to be highly influential in the 3D scene generation field. It provides a necessary overview, aiding both established researchers and newcomers. It should contribute towards a more unified understanding of the landscape and encourage research along the outlined future directions.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper's comprehensive scope and structured organization are its major strengths, warranting a high score. The detailed overview of datasets, evaluation metrics, and applications further enhances its value. The clear articulation of challenges and future research directions offers a helpful guide to the community. While the survey doesn't delve deeply into every technical detail, its breadth of coverage and its critical analysis of different approaches make it a significant contribution. The weaknesses regarding depth and the inherent subjectivity of categorization are relatively minor compared to the overall value provided. The constantly updated project page solidifies its importance and long-term impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### SVAD: From Single Image to 3D Avatar via Synthetic Data Generation with Video Diffusion and Data Augmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05475v1)
- **Authors**: Yonwoo Choi
- **Abstract**: Creating high-quality animatable 3D human avatars from a single image remains a significant challenge in computer vision due to the inherent difficulty of reconstructing complete 3D information from a single viewpoint. Current approaches face a clear limitation: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) methods produce high-quality results but require multiple views or video sequences, while video diffusion models can generate animations from single images but struggle with consistency and identity preservation. We present SVAD, a novel approach that addresses these limitations by leveraging complementary strengths of existing techniques. Our method generates synthetic training data through video diffusion, enhances it with identity preservation and image restoration modules, and utilizes this refined data to train 3DGS avatars. Comprehensive evaluations demonstrate that SVAD outperforms state-of-the-art (SOTA) single-image methods in maintaining identity consistency and fine details across novel poses and viewpoints, while enabling real-time rendering capabilities. Through our data augmentation pipeline, we overcome the dependency on dense monocular or multi-view training data typically required by traditional 3DGS approaches. Extensive quantitative, qualitative comparisons show our method achieves superior performance across multiple metrics against baseline models. By effectively combining the generative power of diffusion models with both the high-quality results and rendering efficiency of 3DGS, our work establishes a new approach for high-fidelity avatar generation from a single image input.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SVAD: From Single Image to 3D Avatar via Synthetic Data Generation with Video Diffusion and Data Augmentation" presents a novel pipeline, SVAD, for creating high-fidelity, animatable 3D human avatars from a single image. The core idea is to generate synthetic training data using a pose-conditioned video diffusion model. This synthetic data is then enhanced using identity preservation and image restoration modules.  Finally, the refined data is used to train a 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) avatar. The method aims to combine the generative power of diffusion models with the efficient rendering capabilities of 3DGS. Experiments demonstrate that SVAD outperforms state-of-the-art single-image avatar generation methods in terms of identity consistency, detail preservation, and real-time rendering.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of existing techniques. Video diffusion models have been used for animation, and 3DGS has been used for avatar creation from multi-view data.  The core contribution is in the pipeline that leverages video diffusion to *synthesize training data* for 3DGS from a single image.  This is a smart solution that addresses a key limitation of 3DGS: its reliance on multi-view or video input. The use of identity preservation and image restoration modules to refine the diffusion outputs adds another layer of contribution, although the specific techniques used are arguably incremental improvements. The integration of a strong SOTA diffusion model and adapting the process to training the Gausian model provides an incremental update to the overall pipeline.

*   **Significance:** The ability to create high-quality, animatable 3D avatars from a single image has significant practical applications. The paper addresses a key challenge in the field and offers a viable solution with good performance. The real-time rendering capability afforded by 3DGS is also a significant advantage. The reported improvements over existing methods are substantial, suggesting a genuine advancement in the state-of-the-art.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The approach is technically sound and well-motivated.
    *   The paper presents a comprehensive pipeline with clear descriptions of each component.
    *   Extensive experiments demonstrate superior performance compared to existing methods.
    *   The use of 3DGS enables real-time rendering, a crucial factor for many applications.
    *   The method clearly addresses the identity preservation challenges typically associated with video diffusion.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a pre-trained video diffusion model limits the method's flexibility and generality. If the diffusion model struggles with certain poses, clothing types, or subject appearances, SVAD will inherit these limitations.
    *   The running time of ~6 hours per avatar is relatively high, limiting its practicality for some applications. Further optimization is necessary.
    *   The failure cases discussed in the paper highlight some of the limitations of the approach, particularly with segmentation, loose clothing, and back view synthesis.
    *   While the individual modules (identity preservation, image restoration) are beneficial, they are not groundbreaking in and of themselves. Their effectiveness stems from their integration within the overall pipeline.
    *   The choice of evaluating primarily on the People Snapshot dataset while common, creates some concerns about generalizability.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in 3D avatar generation by demonstrating the effectiveness of synthetic data generation using video diffusion models. It could inspire new approaches for addressing the limitations of 3DGS and other 3D reconstruction techniques. The pipeline's modular design makes it adaptable, offering possibilities for refinement through upgrades to each module to further improve results.

**Justification for the Score:**

SVAD represents a solid contribution to the field of 3D avatar generation. While it builds upon existing techniques, the specific combination and application to generate training data for 3DGS from a single image is novel and effective. The method addresses a key limitation of 3DGS and achieves state-of-the-art performance. However, the reliance on pre-trained models, relatively high running time, and some remaining limitations prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking advancement. A score of 7 reflects the paper's genuine novelty, significant practical implications, and well-supported experimental results, while acknowledging its limitations and incremental nature.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
